<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a smooth metric on the three-sphere, arbitrarily close to the round metric in the smooth topology, and a smooth metric on $`S^2\times\mathbb T^2`$ for which sequences of exact real Laplace eigenfunctions have unbounded nodal measure divided by the square root of the eigenvalue. Each sequence belongs to one fixed metric. Thus the upper-bound part of Yau's nodal conjecture fails for smooth metrics in dimensions three and four.

- **claim-main-three-sphere** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $g_*$ be the round metric on $\mathbb S^3$. Every neighborhood of $g_*$ in the $C^\infty$ topology contains a smooth Riemannian metric $g_\infty$ for which there are nonzero smooth real functions $u_j$ and positive numbers $\lambda_j$ satisfying $$-\Delta_{g_\infty}u_j=\lambda_j u_j,\qquad \lambda_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad \frac{\mathcal H^2_{g_\infty}(Z_{u_j})}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}} \longrightarrow\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Arbitrarily close to the round metric on the three-sphere there is one smooth metric carrying eigenfunctions whose nodal area divided by the square root of the eigenvalue is unbounded.
- **claim-four-manifold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-four)): There are a smooth Riemannian metric $g_\infty$ on $S^2\times\mathbb T^2$, nonzero smooth real functions $u_j$, and positive numbers $\lambda_j\to\infty$ such that $$-\Delta_{g_\infty}u_j=\lambda_j u_j,\qquad \frac{\mathcal H^3_{g_\infty}(Z_{u_j})}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}} \longrightarrow\infty.$$
  - Explanation: On the product of a two-sphere and a two-torus there is one smooth metric with eigenfunctions whose normalized nodal measure is unbounded.
- **claim-profile-amplification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-profile-amplification)): Let $\Omega$ be a coordinate cube with compact closure in a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Suppose that $\phi$ is smooth on a neighborhood of $\overline\Omega$, is strict there, and satisfies $a_\phi\ne0$ there. Given $T>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth function $\widehat\phi$ on the same neighborhood such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\widehat\phi-\phi)\Subset\Omega,\qquad \\|\widehat\phi-\phi\\|_{C^0(\Omega)}<\varepsilon, \qquad \int_\Omega \|a_{\widehat\phi}\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g>T.$$ The function $\widehat\phi$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\overline\Omega$.* *More precisely, there is a constant $\eta>0$, determined only by the fixed periodic functions chosen below, such that one finite cycle of the construction multiplies $\int_\Omega\|a_\phi\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$ by at least $1+\eta$. The cycle can have arbitrarily small $C^0$ size. Its patch sizes and frequency thresholds may depend on the entire incoming profile and metric.
  - Explanation: A strict profile with nonvanishing gradient can be changed by an arbitrarily small compactly supported perturbation so that its gradient integral exceeds any prescribed level while strictness is kept.
- **claim-disjoint-patches-averaging** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-localization)): Let $U$ be an open subset of a relatively compact coordinate cube, and let $\mu$ be a finite measure given by a positive smooth density.* 1. *Given an open neighborhood of each point of $U$, one can choose finitely many coordinate cubes $P_j$, with disjoint interiors and closures contained in those neighborhoods and in $U$, and functions $0\le\chi_j\le1$ in $C_c^\infty(P_j)$, such that $\chi_j=1$ on open cores $C_j\Subset P_j$ and $$\mu\Bigl(\bigcup_j C_j\Bigr)\ge\frac12\mu(U).$$ In particular, the supports of the $\chi_j$ are pairwise disjoint.* 2. *Suppose that $y$ is a smooth submersion to $\mathbb R^q$, with $q=1$ or $2$, on a neighborhood of $\overline P$, and that $C\Subset P$ is measurable. Let $E\subset\mathbb R^q$ be periodic with respect to a full lattice, have boundary of measure zero in a period cell, and occupy a fraction $p$ of that cell. Then $$\lim_{N\to\infty} \mu\{x\in C:Ny(x)\in E\}=p\,\mu(C).$$
  - Explanation: Disjoint coordinate cubes with cutoff cores capture at least half of a smooth measure, and periodic sets equidistribute under a submersion at high frequency.
- **claim-profile-preparation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-preparation)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-amplification">4</a>, let $0<\epsilon_p\le1/3$ and $\varepsilon>0$. There is a smooth strict function $\psi$, with nonvanishing gradient on $\overline\Omega$, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\psi-\phi)\Subset\Omega,\qquad \\|\psi-\phi\\|_{C^0(\Omega)}<\varepsilon, \qquad \|a_\psi-a_\phi\|\le\epsilon_p\|a_\phi\| \quad\hbox{on }\overline\Omega.$$ Its full region $U\subset\Omega$ satisfies $$\int_U\|a_\psi\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g \ge\frac{p_h}{8}\int_\Omega\|a_\psi\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g. \label{eq:prepared-mass}$$ The change $\psi-\phi$ can, in addition, be made arbitrarily small in $C^1$.
  - Explanation: A one-variable oscillation makes the profile full on a fixed fraction of the gradient mass while changing it little in the supremum and relative gradient senses.
- **claim-radial-corrugation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-radial-corrugation)): There are constants $\delta>0$ and $g_0>0$, depending only on the fixed function $F$, with the following property. Let $\psi$ be full with nonvanishing gradient on a neighborhood of the closure of a relatively compact coordinate patch $P$. Suppose that $y=(y_1,y_2)$ is a smooth submersion there, with $$\,\mathrm dy(a_\psi)=0, \qquad\text{both nonzero singular values of }\,\mathrm dy \text{ in }[1/2,2].$$ Let $A>0$ be constant and satisfy $1/2\le A/\|a_\psi\|\le2$, and let $0\le\chi\le1$ belong to $C_c^\infty(P)$. Set $$\Phi_N=\psi+\frac{\delta A}{N}\chi F(Ny).$$ For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the function $\Phi_N$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\overline P$. Moreover, $\\|\Phi_N-\psi\\|_{C^0(P)}\to0$. Given any $\epsilon_r>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \|a_{\Phi_N}\|&\ge(1-\epsilon_r)\|a_\psi\| &&\text{on }\overline P,\label{eq:radial-no-loss}\\ \|a_{\Phi_N}\|&\ge(1+g_0-\epsilon_r)\|a_\psi\| &&\text{where }\chi=1\text{ and }Ny\in E_F. \label{eq:radial-gain} \end{aligned}$$ The frequency thresholds may depend on all the fixed patch data and on $\epsilon_r$; the constants $\delta,g_0$ do not.
  - Explanation: A radial corrugation in two transverse variables raises the gradient by a definite factor on an annular set, loses almost nothing elsewhere and preserves strictness.
- **claim-spherical-profiles** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-spherical-profiles)): There is a $C^2$ neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $g_*$ such that the following holds. For every smooth $g\in\mathcal U$, every $1/5\le t\le3/10$, and every $T,\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth real function $\phi$ on $\mathbb S^3$ with $$\phi_0=\log t+r^2-t^2,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi-\phi_0)\Subset\Omega, \qquad \\|\phi-\phi_0\\|_{C^0}<\varepsilon,$$ such that* 1. *$\phi$ is strict on $r\le3/10$;* 2. *its critical points in this region are exactly $r=0$, and at each such point $H_\phi$ is positive on a two-plane;* 3. *$\phi>\log r$ for $0<r<t$, while $\phi<\log r$ for $t<r\le1$;* 4. *$\displaystyle\int_\Omega \|a_\phi\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g>T$.* *The metric-coordinate comparison constants on $\Omega$, including the Lipschitz constants of its two-coordinate projections for the ambient Riemannian distance, can be chosen uniformly for $g\in\mathcal U$. In particular, $$\int_\Omega L_\phi\,\,\mathrm dx \ge c\int_\Omega \|a_\phi\|\,\,\mathrm dV_g \label{eq:profile-coordinate-mass}$$ with one constant $c>0$ for this neighborhood. Each constructed profile is fixed before a wave frequency is chosen.
  - Explanation: On the round sphere and nearby metrics there are strict global profiles with prescribed radial behavior, controlled critical points and arbitrarily large gradient integral.
- **claim-admissible-phase-hessian** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-phase-hessian)): Let $H$ be a real symmetric form on a three-dimensional Euclidean space, and let $z=a+ib$, where $a,b$ are real, $a\perp b$, and $\|b\|^2=1+\|a\|^2$. If $a\ne0$ and $H(a,a)+H(b,b)>0$, there is a complex symmetric form $Q$ such that $$Qz=0,\qquad \Re Q<H. \label{eq:phase-hessian-conditions}$$ If $a=0$, the same conclusion follows from $H(b,b)>0$. Moreover, a choice satisfying <a href="#eq:phase-hessian-conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:phase-hessian-conditions">[eq:phase-hessian-conditions]</a> continues with locally bounded coefficients and a positive real-part gap under small changes of $H,z$ with $z\cdot z=-1$.
  - Explanation: Given the strictness inequality there is a complex symmetric form annihilating the complex phase gradient whose real part lies strictly below the real Hessian, with continuation under small changes.
- **claim-localized-finite-waves** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-waves)): For every pair of nonnegative integers $m,D$, there are common normal coordinate balls about the points of $K_1$ and three waves at every center $p\in K_1$, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, with the following properties. Each wave is a smooth complex function on the sphere supported in $r<t_2$ and has the form $$Z=\zeta e^{nS}V, \qquad V=\sum_{j=0}^J n^{-j}V_j, \label{eq:finite-wave-form}$$ in its center’s normal coordinates. Here $S,V_j$ are complex polynomials and $\zeta$ is a smooth cutoff equal to one on a common smaller ball. With $\delta=n^{-1/2}$, the three phases have initial data $$S(p)=\phi(p),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{grad}}S(p)=z_\ell, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}S(p)=Q_\ell,$$ as in <a href="#eq:three-phase-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:three-phase-data">[eq:three-phase-data]</a>–<a href="#eq:uniform-phase-gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-phase-gap">[eq:uniform-phase-gap]</a>. Their amplitudes satisfy $V_0(p)=1$ and $V_j(p)=0$ for $j>0$. On their supports, $$\Re S(x)-\phi(x) \le n^{-1/2}\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)-c\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)^2, \label{eq:wave-localization}$$ with a common $c>0$. Setting $\Lambda_n=n(n+2)$, one has $$\bigl\|\nabla^j(\Delta_g+\Lambda_n)Z(x)\bigr\|_g \le Cn^{-D}e^{n\phi(x)},\qquad 0\le j\le m. \label{eq:wave-individual-residual}$$ For any further fixed finite collection of derivative orders, the construction can also be required to satisfy $$\|\nabla^jZ(x)\|_g\le C_j n^j e^{n\phi(x)} \label{eq:wave-individual-derivatives}$$ at those orders. All the constants, polynomial degrees, and ball radii are fixed before $n$; they may depend on $g,\phi,m,D$ and the further derivative orders.
  - Explanation: Localized complex waves with polynomial phase and amplitude have weighted residual smaller than any prescribed inverse power of the frequency and Gaussian localization under the envelope.
- **claim-sign-functional-area** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-sign-area)): The functional $\mathcal F_n$ is lower semicontinuous for uniform convergence of real functions. It is unchanged by multiplication by a nonzero real constant or by an everywhere positive continuous function. There is a constant $C_{\rm area}$, depending only on the fixed coordinates and metric neighborhood, such that for every continuous real $f$ and every $h\in\mathcal G$, $$\mathcal F_n(f)\le C_{\rm area}\mathcal H_h^2(\{f=0\}). \label{eq:sign-area-bound}$$ In particular $C_{\rm area}$ is independent of $\phi,\mathscr P$, and $n$. There is also a constant $C_{\rm sign}$ with the same independence such that $$0\le\mathcal F_n(f)\le C_{\rm sign}n\int_\Omega L(x)\,dx. \label{eq:sign-deterministic-upper}$$
  - Explanation: The sign functional is lower semicontinuous, scale invariant and bounded by a constant times the nodal Hausdorff area with no regularity of the zero set assumed.
- **claim-real-jet-noncancellation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-wave-noncancellation)): Fix any nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. Construct the waves with those residual requirements and with all derivative bounds needed through order $\max(m_*,2)$. There is a fixed exponent $B=110$, independent of the profile and these orders, such that with probability tending to one as $n\to\infty$, $$\|U_n\|+n^{-1}\|\nabla U_n\|_g\ge n^{-B}W_n \quad\hbox{everywhere on the sphere}, \label{eq:wave-jet-lower}$$ and simultaneously $$\begin{split} \|\nabla^jU_n\|_g&\le C_j n^{j+4}W_n,\\ \|\nabla^j(\Delta_g+\Lambda_n)U_n\|_g &\le C n^{4-D_*}W_n, \qquad 0\le j\le m_*. \end{split} \label{eq:wave-global-estimates}$$ The constants and the rate of convergence of the probability may depend on the fixed metric, profile, and orders, but not on $n$.
  - Explanation: A Gaussian superposition of the localized waves plus a harmonic background has value and gradient that do not vanish together, with a fixed exponent, with high probability.
- **claim-sign-expectation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-wave-sign-expectation)): With a fixed sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$ as above, there is a constant $c_{\rm sign}>0$, independent of the fixed profile and of the wave accuracy orders, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\mathbb E\mathcal F_n(U_n) \ge c_{\rm sign} n\int_\Omega L(x)\,dx. \label{eq:sign-expectation-lower}$$ The threshold for $n$ may depend on the entire fixed construction.
  - Explanation: The expected sign functional of the random superposition grows linearly in the frequency with a constant independent of the profile.
- **claim-wave-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-wave-realization)): Under the hypotheses of this section, fix arbitrary nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. For every sufficiently large integer $n$ there is a smooth real $U_n$ satisfying <a href="#eq:wave-jet-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:wave-jet-lower">[eq:wave-jet-lower]</a> and <a href="#eq:wave-global-estimates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:wave-global-estimates">[eq:wave-global-estimates]</a>, with $B=110$, and $$\mathcal F_n(U_n)\ge c_{\rm wave} n\int_\Omega L(x)\,dx, \label{eq:wave-sign-lower}$$ where $c_{\rm wave}>0$ depends only on the fixed coordinates and metric neighborhood. Moreover, $$U_n=w_n\quad\hbox{and}\quad g=g_* \qquad\hbox{on }\{r\ge t_2\}. \label{eq:wave-clean-exterior}$$ The thresholds and constants in the differential estimates may depend on the fixed profile and accuracy orders; $B$ and $c_{\rm wave}$ do not. The sign functional is finite, fixed, and has all the persistence and area-comparison properties of Proposition <a href="#prop:sign-functional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sign-functional">[prop:sign-functional]</a>.
  - Explanation: One realization of the superposition simultaneously satisfies the noncancellation bound, the weighted derivative and residual bounds, a large sign functional and an unchanged exterior.
- **claim-scalar-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-quotients)): Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth Riemannian manifold. Fix an integer $B\ge1$, and let $h\ge0$ and $D_*>0$ be integers. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, a real smooth function $U=U_n$ and a positive continuous function $W=W_n$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \|U\|+n^{-1}\|\nabla U\|&\ge n^{-B}W, \label{eq:exact-jet-hypothesis}\\ \|\nabla^jU\|&\le C_j n^{j+4}W, &\|\nabla^j\mathcal R\|&\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W \quad (0\le j\le h+2), \label{eq:exact-weighted-hypothesis} \end{aligned}$$ where $\mathcal R=(\Delta_g+\Lambda)U$ and the constants $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Define $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal D&=n^2U^2+\|\nabla U\|^2,\qquad b=1-\frac{\mathcal R U}{\mathcal D},\\ s&=1-\Lambda^{-1}\left[ \frac{\mathcal R n^2U}{\mathcal D} -\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g\left(\frac{\mathcal R}{\mathcal D}\nabla U\right)\right]. \end{aligned} \label{eq:exact-scalar-correction}$$ Then $b,s$ are smooth, and, with $\Delta_b f=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g(b\nabla f)$, $$\Delta_bU+\Lambda sU=0. \label{eq:exact-weighted-equation}$$ They equal one on every open set where $\mathcal R=0$. Moreover, if $$E=2B+20,\qquad Q_h=(E+1)(h+2)+10,$$ then $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^h}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^h} \le C_h n^{-D_*+Q_h}. \label{eq:exact-quotient-bound}$$ In particular, for any prescribed integer $P_1\ge1$, choosing $D_*\ge P_1+Q_h+2$ makes the left side at most $n^{-P_1}$ for all sufficiently large $n$. In this case $b$ and $s$ are positive for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: Explicit smooth conductivity and density coefficients absorb the residual exactly, and they are close to one when the residual accuracy is high.
- **claim-metric-conductivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-metric-conductivity)): Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold and let $A$ be a smooth positive $g$-self-adjoint endomorphism of $TM$. Put $d=\det A$, with determinant taken in any $g$-orthonormal frame, and define $$g_A(X,Y)=g(dA^{-1}X,Y). \label{eq:exact-metric-realization}$$ Then $g_A$ is a smooth Riemannian metric and $$\,\mathrm dV_{g_A}=d\,\,\mathrm dV_g,\qquad \Delta_{g_A}f=d^{-1}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g(A\nabla f). \label{eq:exact-metric-operator}$$
  - Explanation: In dimension three a positive self-adjoint endomorphism defines a metric whose volume density and Laplacian realize the prescribed conductivity.
- **claim-positive-conjugating-factor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-positive-factor)): Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Given integers $k_2\ge0$ and $P_2\ge1$, choose an even integer $l\ge4$ with $l>k_2+3/2$, and set $$k_1=l,\qquad P_1=P_2+8.$$ Suppose that smooth functions $b=b_n$ and $s=s_n$ satisfy $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^{k_1}} \le n^{-P_1}. \label{eq:exact-coefficient-smallness}$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, there is a positive smooth function $v=v_n$ solving $$-\Delta_bv+\Lambda b^3v^5-\Lambda sv=0 \label{eq:exact-semilinear}$$ and satisfying $$\\|v-1\\|_{C^{k_2}}\le n^{-P_2}. \label{eq:exact-factor-bound}$$ The required threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed manifold and the displayed finite orders.
  - Explanation: A semilinear elliptic equation has a positive smooth solution close to one, obtained by a contraction argument in Sobolev spaces uniformly in the frequency.
- **claim-exactification** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-exactification)): Let $g$ be a fixed smooth metric on $\mathbb S^3$, and fix $0<t_2<t_3<1$. Assume that $g=g_*$ on $\{r\ge t_2\}$, where $g_*$ is the unit round metric and $r=(x_1^2+x_2^2)^{1/2}$. Fix an integer $B\ge1$.* *For every pair of integers $k\ge0$, $P\ge1$, there exist finite integers $m_*,D_*$, depending only on $k,P,B$, with the following property. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, there are real smooth functions $U_n$ and positive continuous functions $W_n$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \|U_n\|+n^{-1}\|\nabla U_n\|\ge n^{-B}W_n, \label{eq:exact-proposition-lower}\\ \|\nabla^jU_n\|\le C_jn^{j+4}W_n,\qquad \|\nabla^j((\Delta_g+\Lambda_n)U_n)\| \le C_jn^{4-D_*}W_n \quad(0\le j\le m_*), \label{eq:exact-proposition-upper}\\ U_n=w_n=\Re(x_1+ix_2)^n\quad\hbox{on }\{r\ge t_2\}, \label{eq:exact-proposition-exterior} \end{gathered}$$ where all $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, there are a smooth Riemannian metric $\widehat g_n$ and a positive smooth function $\widetilde v_n$ on $\mathbb S^3$ such that, with $u_n=U_n/\widetilde v_n$, $$\begin{gathered} (\Delta_{\widehat g_n}+\Lambda_n)u_n=0, \qquad \\|\widehat g_n-g\\|_{C^k}\le n^{-P}, \label{eq:exact-final-equation}\\ \widehat g_n=g_*,\quad \widetilde v_n=1,\quad u_n=w_n \quad\hbox{on }\{r\ge t_3\}. \label{eq:exact-final-exterior} \end{gathered}$$ In particular $u_n$ is nonzero and has exactly the same pointwise signs and zero set as $U_n$. The threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed input data and on the constants in <a href="#eq:exact-proposition-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exact-proposition-upper">[eq:exact-proposition-upper]</a>.
  - Explanation: The approximate wave becomes an exact eigenfunction of a nearby smooth metric with the same signs and zero set and an unchanged round exterior.
- **claim-nondegenerate-pencil** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-nondegenerate-pencil)): Let $V$ be a finite-dimensional real vector space and let $\mathcal L\subset\operatorname{Sym}^2(V^*)$ be a linear subspace. Suppose that, for each $v\ne0$, some $B\in\mathcal L$ satisfies $B(v,v)\ne0$. Then $\mathcal L$ contains a nondegenerate form.
  - Explanation: A linear space of symmetric forms that is nonzero on every nonzero vector contains a nondegenerate form.
- **claim-pinned-simplicity** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pinned-simplicity)): Let $h$ be a smooth metric on $\mathbb S^3$ and let $u$ be a nonzero real smooth function satisfying $$-\Delta_hu=\Lambda_nu,\qquad \Lambda_n=n(n+2),\qquad n\ge2.$$ Suppose that, on an annulus $\mathcal A=\{a<r<b\}$ with $0<a<b<1$, one has $h=g_*$ and $u=w_n$. In every $C^\infty$ neighborhood of $h$ there is a smooth metric $h'$ such that $h'-h$ is supported compactly in $\mathcal A$, $$dV_{h'}=dV_h,\qquad -\Delta_{h'}u=\Lambda_nu,$$ and $\Lambda_n$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\Delta_{h'}$.
  - Explanation: A compactly supported volume-preserving metric change in a round annulus makes the eigenvalue simple while keeping the eigenfunction exactly.
- **claim-simple-eigenpair-continuity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-simple-eigenpair-continuity)): Let $M$ be a closed smooth manifold, let $h_0$ be a smooth Riemannian metric, and suppose that $\lambda_0>0$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\Delta_{h_0}$ with real eigenfunction $u_0$ normalized by $\\|u_0\\|_{L^2(h_0)}=1$. For every open interval $I$ containing $\lambda_0$, there is a $C^\infty$ neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $h_0$ and, for every $h\in\mathcal U$, a simple eigenvalue $\lambda(h)\in I$ with a normalized real eigenfunction $u(h)$. One can choose the signs so that $$\lambda(h)\longrightarrow\lambda_0, \qquad u(h)\longrightarrow u_0\quad\hbox{in }C^\infty(M) \quad\hbox{as }h\longrightarrow h_0.$$ In particular, if $\mathcal F$ is lower semicontinuous on $C^0(M)$, invariant under nonzero real rescaling, and $\mathcal F(u_0)>T$, then $\mathcal U$ can be made small enough that $\mathcal F(u(h))>T$ for all $h\in\mathcal U$.
  - Explanation: A simple eigenpair varies continuously in the smooth topology, so strict lower bounds of a lower semicontinuous scale-invariant functional persist.
- **claim-one-construction-step** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-one-step)): Suppose that $g\in\mathcal V$ agrees with $g_*$ on $r\ge R_0$, where $$\frac15\le R_0<R_1\le\frac3{10}.$$ Let $\mathcal N$ be any $C^\infty$ neighborhood of $g$, let $A>0$, and let $N\ge2$ be an integer. There are an integer $n\ge N$, a metric $g'\in\mathcal N\cap\mathcal V$ agreeing with $g_*$ on $r\ge R_1$, and a nonzero real smooth function $u$ such that $-\Delta_{g'}u=\Lambda_nu$ and $\Lambda_n$ is simple. Moreover, there is an open neighborhood $\mathcal W$ of $g'$ in $\mathcal V$ such that every $h\in\mathcal W$ has an exact real eigenfunction $u_h$ with eigenvalue $\lambda_h$ satisfying $$\label{eq:one-step-open-witness} \Lambda_n-1<\lambda_h<\Lambda_n+1, \qquad \frac{\mathcal H_h^2(\{u_h=0\})}{\sqrt{\lambda_h}}>A.$$ This neighborhood is obtained by retaining one fixed sign functional and one fixed strict numerical threshold.
  - Explanation: Near any admissible metric one finds a metric with a simple eigenvalue and an open set of metrics where the nodal ratio exceeds a given level.
- **claim-background-modes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-env-background-mode)): For every integer $k\ge1$, set $$\Lambda_k=2k^2+k,\qquad u_k^0=\sin^kp\cos(k\phi),\qquad \phi=\theta+z.$$ Then $u_k^0$ is a smooth real eigenfunction on $M$ with $(\Delta_{g_0}+\Lambda_k)u_k^0=0$. Set $f_0=\log\sin p$ on a neighborhood of $\overline P$. There is a constant $c>0$, independent of $k$, such that $$\label{env:background-gradient} \|du_k^0\|_{g_0}\ge ck e^{kf_0}\quad\hbox{on }\overline P.$$
  - Explanation: Explicit background eigenfunctions on the product manifold have a gradient lower bound on the chosen cube, uniform in the frequency index.
- **claim-uniform-amplification** (construction, [source](#stmt-env-amplify)): There is $d_*>0$, depending only on the comparison constant $K$ and fixed auxiliary functions, with the following property. Suppose $h$ is smooth, satisfies <a href="#env:metric-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="env:metric-comparison">[env:metric-comparison]</a> near $\overline D$, and $f$ is a strict envelope for $h$ on a neighborhood of $\overline D$. For every $b>0$ there is a smooth strict envelope $\widetilde f$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \operatorname{supp}(\widetilde f-f)\Subset D,\qquad \\|\widetilde f-f\\|_{L^\infty}<b,\label{env:amplification-smallness}\\ \int_D\|\nabla_h\widetilde f\|_h\,dx \ge(1+d_*)\int_D\|\nabla_h f\|_h\,dx. \label{env:amplification-gain} \end{gathered}$$ Consequently the gradient integral can be made arbitrarily large by a perturbation compactly supported in $D$, with any prescribed positive total sup-norm budget. No derivative bound on the resulting envelope is asserted.
  - Explanation: Corrugations in two directions raise the gradient integral of a strict envelope by a fixed factor with arbitrarily small supremum change.
- **claim-envelope-gaps** (construction, [source](#stmt-env-gaps)): Fix $h\in\mathcal U$ with $\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\Subset P$. There are nested open coordinate boxes $$\overline D\cup\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\subset U_0 \Subset U_1\Subset U_2\Subset U_3\Subset U_4\Subset P.$$ For every $L_*>0$ there is a smooth strict envelope $f$ for $h$ on a neighborhood of $\overline P$, and a number $\sigma>0$, such that $$\begin{gathered} f>f_0+\sigma\quad\hbox{on }\overline U_0, \qquad f<f_0-\sigma\quad\hbox{on }\overline P\setminus U_1, \label{env:gap-inequalities}\\ \int_D\sqrt{2+\|\nabla_h f\|_h^2}\,dx>L_*. \label{env:large-integral} \end{gathered}$$ The envelope and all these patches are fixed before the frequency $k$ is chosen.
  - Explanation: There is a strict envelope with arbitrarily large integral that lies above the background profile on an inner box and below it away from a slightly larger box.
- **claim-finite-order-packets** (construction, [source](#stmt-wave-packets)): For any integers $\ell,J\ge0$, and all sufficiently large integers $k$, there are packets indexed by $$Y_k=(k^{-1}\mathbb Z^4)\cap U_3,\qquad y\in Y_k,\quad 1\le j\le3,$$ of the form $$p_{yj,k}(x)=e^{k\Phi_{yj}(x)}A_{yj,k}(x)\xi(x-y). \label{wave:packet-form}$$ Here $\xi$ is a smooth cutoff supported in a fixed small ball, equal to one in a smaller ball; all translated supporting balls lie in $P$. The phases are complex polynomials in $x-y$, and the amplitudes are polynomials of fixed finite degree in $x-y$ and $k^{-1}$. Their coefficients are uniformly bounded over the centers and indices. They satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \Phi_{yj}(y)&=f(y),& d\Phi_{yj}(y)&=df(y)+i b_j^\flat,\notag\\ \Re\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_h\Phi_{yj}(y)&=H_y-c_{yj}h_y,& A_{yj,k}(y)&=1. \label{wave:packet-jets} \end{aligned}$$ On each supporting ball, for positive constants $c,C$, $$-C\|x-y\|^2\le\Re\Phi_{yj}(x)-f(x) \le-c\|x-y\|^2, \qquad A_{yj,k}(x)=1+O(\|x-y\|+k^{-1}). \label{wave:gaussian-bounds}$$ The amplitude and each of its fixed-order derivatives are uniformly bounded. For every fixed multi-index $\alpha$, $$\|\partial^\alpha p_{yj,k}(x)\| \le C_\alpha k^{\|\alpha\|} e^{kf(x)-ck\|x-y\|^2}, \label{wave:packet-derivatives}$$ after decreasing $c$ if necessary, and $$\sup_{x\in P}e^{-kf(x)} \sum_{\|\alpha\|\le\ell} \big\|\partial^\alpha(\Delta_h+\Lambda_k)p_{yj,k}(x)\big\| \le C_{\ell,J}k^{-J}. \label{wave:packet-residual}$$ The radii, coefficients, constants, and frequency threshold are fixed before $k$ varies.
  - Explanation: Gaussian-localized complex packets are built on a fine grid with polynomial phase and amplitude and arbitrarily small weighted residual.
- **claim-weighted-residual-four** (bound, [source](#stmt-wave-residual)): For any predetermined integers $\ell,J\ge0$, choose the packets of Proposition <a href="#wave:packets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="wave:packets">28</a> with derivative order $\ell$ and residual power $J+6$. On $\mathcal E_k$, the function in <a href="#wave:random" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wave:random">[wave:random]</a> equals $u_k^0$ outside a compact subset of $U_2$, its residual $R=(\Delta_h+\Lambda_k)u$ is compactly supported in $U_2$, and $$\begin{aligned} \sup_P W^{-1}\|\partial^\alpha u\| &\le C_\alpha k^{\|\alpha\|+5} &&\text{for every fixed }\alpha,\label{wave:u-derivatives}\\ \sup_P W^{-1}\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha R\| &\le C_{\ell,J}k^{-J}. \label{wave:weighted-residual} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, the exponents in <a href="#wave:u-derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wave:u-derivatives">[wave:u-derivatives]</a> are independent of $\ell,J$.
  - Explanation: The random superposition has bounded derivatives with fixed exponents and a weighted residual that decays at any requested power.
- **claim-real-noncancellation-four** (bound, [source](#stmt-wave-noncancellation)): For every fixed choice of the preceding finite accuracy orders, the event $$\mathcal G_k=\mathcal E_k\cap \left\{\, \|(u(x),k^{-1}du(x))\| \ge k^{-200}W(x) \text{ for every }x\in\overline U_2\,\right\} \label{wave:good-event}$$ has probability tending to one as $k\to\infty$. The norm in this formula is the Euclidean norm on the value and four coordinate derivatives. The power $200$ is independent of the requested accuracy.
  - Explanation: Five independent real Gaussian directions give a value-gradient lower bound with high probability, with a fixed exponent independent of accuracy.
- **claim-score-bounds-nodal-measure** (bound, [source](#stmt-score-measure)): There is $c_{\mathrm H}>0$, independent of the subdivision, $k$, and the envelope, such that, for every $g\in\mathcal U$ and every real $\psi\in C(M)$, $$\label{score:hausdorff} \mathcal H_g^3(\{\psi=0\})\ge c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(\psi).$$
  - Explanation: The sign score bounds the nodal three-dimensional Hausdorff measure from below by a constant independent of the subdivision, frequency and envelope.
- **claim-expected-score** (bound, [source](#stmt-score-expectation)): One can choose $c_0>0$ and $c_{\mathrm E}>0$, using only uniform metric comparability, such that the Gaussian superposition <a href="#wave:random" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="wave:random">[wave:random]</a> satisfies, for all sufficiently large integers $k$, $$\label{score:expected} \mathbb E\mathcal S(u)\ge c_{\mathrm E}T_k.$$ The threshold for $k$ may depend on all fixed data, including the envelope and the packet accuracy, whereas $c_0,c_{\mathrm E}$ do not.
  - Explanation: The Gaussian superposition has expected sign score at least a constant multiple of the total grid weight.
- **claim-good-realization-four** (construction, [source](#stmt-score-good)): For every predetermined pair of finite orders $\ell,J\ge0$, all sufficiently large integers $k$ admit a choice of the real Gaussian coefficients for which the smooth real function $u$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} &u=u_k^0\quad\text{outside a compact subset of }U_2, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}R\Subset U_2, \qquad R=(\Delta_h+\Lambda_k)u,\label{score:support}\\ &\sup_P W^{-1}\sum_{\|\alpha\|\le\ell}\|\partial^\alpha R\| \le C_{\ell,J}k^{-J},\label{score:residual}\\ &W^{-1}\|\partial^\alpha u\|\le C_\alpha k^{\|\alpha\|+5} \quad\text{on }P\text{ for every fixed multi-index }\alpha,\notag\\ &\|(u,k^{-1}du)\|\ge k^{-200}W \quad\text{on }\overline U_2,\label{score:bounds}\\ &\mathcal S(u)\ge c_{\mathrm s}k\int_D B(x)\,dx. \label{score:large} \end{aligned}$$ Here $c_{\mathrm s}>0$ is independent of the envelope and the accuracy orders. The constants $C_{\ell,J},C_\alpha$ and the frequency threshold may depend on these fixed data, but the polynomial exponents in these bounds do not increase with the residual accuracy.
  - Explanation: Some choice of coefficients gives support, residual, derivative and noncancellation bounds together with a large sign score.
- **claim-ordinary-metric-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-corr-realization)): Fix the background $M,g_0,P,u_k^0,\Lambda_k$ of Lemma <a href="#env:background-mode" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="env:background-mode">25</a>, patches $U_2\Subset U_3\Subset U_4\Subset P$, a smooth metric $h$ such that $h-g_0$ has compact support in $U_2$, and a fixed smooth function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\overline P$. Put $$W=e^{kf}+e^{kf_0}.$$ For every integer $p\ge0$ and every $L>0$, there are finite integers $\ell,J$ with the following property. Suppose smooth real functions $u=u_k$, for arbitrarily large integers $k$, satisfy $$\begin{aligned} &\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(u-u_k^0)\Subset U_2, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}R\Subset U_2, \qquad R=(\Delta_h+\Lambda_k)u, \label{corr:support}\tag{C1}\\ &W^{-1}\|\partial^\alpha u\| \le C_\alpha k^{\|\alpha\|+5} \quad\hbox{on }P,\quad \|\alpha\|\le\ell+1, \label{corr:derivatives}\tag{C2}\\ &\bigl(u^2+k^{-2}\|du\|_{\mathrm{Euc}}^2\bigr)^{1/2} \ge k^{-200}W \quad\hbox{on }\overline U_2,\label{corr:noncancellation}\tag{C3}\\ &W^{-1}\|\partial^\alpha R\| \le C_R k^{-J} \quad\hbox{on }P,\quad \|\alpha\|\le\ell. \label{corr:residual}\tag{C4} \end{aligned}$$ The constants in these estimates are independent of $k$; the supports in <a href="#corr:support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="corr:support">[corr:support]</a> need not be independent of $k$. For all sufficiently large such $k$, there are a smooth ordinary metric $g_*$ and a smooth positive function $b_*$ on $M$ such that $$\\|g_*-h\\|_{C^p}=O(k^{-L}),\qquad t=b_*u,\qquad (\Delta_{g_*}+\Lambda_k)t=0.$$ Moreover, $g_*-g_0$ and $t-u_k^0$ have compact support in $U_4$. In particular, the zero set and every strict sign comparison of $u$ are preserved.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently accurate quasimode becomes an exact eigenfunction of an ordinary smooth metric very close to the given one, preserving signs and zero set.
- **claim-weighted-correction** (bound, [source](#stmt-corr-weighted)): Under <a href="#corr:support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="corr:support">[corr:support]</a>–<a href="#corr:residual" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="corr:residual">[corr:residual]</a>, there are a smooth symmetric contravariant tensor $F$ and a smooth scalar $\vartheta$, both compactly supported in $U_2$, such that $$\label{corr:weighted-equation} \mathop{\mathrm{div}}_h\bigl((h^{-1}+F)\,du\bigr) +\Lambda_k(1+\vartheta)u=0.$$ For each integer $0\le r\le\ell-1$, their estimates through order $r$ use only derivatives of $u$ through order $r+2$ and of $R$ through order $r+1$. A fixed finite exponent $E_r$, independent of $J$, satisfies $$\label{corr:weighted-estimate} \\|F\\|_{C^r}+\\|\vartheta\\|_{C^r} \le C_r k^{E_r-J}; \qquad E_r=1000(r+2)\quad\hbox{is sufficient}.$$
  - Explanation: A tensor and a density increment absorb the residual exactly, with norms bounded by a fixed power of the frequency times the residual accuracy.
- **claim-conformal-factor** (construction, [source](#stmt-corr-conformal)): Fix the smooth metric $h$ on the closed four-manifold $M$, and an even integer $s>2$. There are $\delta>0$ and $C_s$, depending only on $h,s$ and the fixed Sobolev norms, with the following property. If $h_1,V_0$ are smooth, $$\\|h_1-h\\|_{C^s}+\\|V_0\\|_{H^s}\le\delta, \qquad \Lambda\ge1,$$ then $$\Delta_{h_1}Z=V_0Z+\Lambda(Z^3-Z)$$ has a smooth positive solution satisfying $$\label{corr:Z-smallness} \\|Z-1\\|_{H^s}\le C_s\Lambda^{-1}\\|V_0\\|_{H^s}.$$
  - Explanation: The semilinear equation with coercive linearization has a smooth positive solution close to one in a Sobolev norm.
- **claim-determinant-repair-collar** (construction, [source](#stmt-corr-localization)): For the data <a href="#corr:localized-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="corr:localized-data">[corr:localized-data]</a>, suppose that $\widehat Z-1$ is sufficiently small in $C^1$ and $\rho>0$. Then an ordinary smooth metric $g_*$, equal to $g_0$ outside a compact subset of $U_4$, satisfies $(\Delta_{g_*}+\Lambda_k)t=0$. More quantitatively, if for some $N>0$, $$\label{corr:collar-input-estimate} \\|h_1-h\\|_{C^p}+\\|V_0\\|_{C^p}=O(k^{-N}), \qquad \\|Z-1\\|_{C^{p+2}}=O(k^{-N-2}),$$ then $$\label{corr:final-metric-estimate} \\|g_*-h\\|_{C^p} =O(k^{-N}+k^{p-N-2}).$$
  - Explanation: A change tangent to the level sets on a clean collar enforces the four-dimensional determinant identity without changing the eigenfunction flux.
- **claim-simplicity-four** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-simple)): Let $g$ be a smooth metric on $M$, and suppose $$-\Delta_g t=\Lambda_k t,\qquad k\ge1,$$ $$g=g_0,\quad t=u_k^0 \quad\hbox{outside a compact subset of }P.$$ In every smooth-topology neighborhood of $g$ there is a smooth metric $\widetilde g$ for which $\Lambda_k$ is simple, with the same eigenfunction $t$. Moreover, $\widetilde g-g$ has compact support in $P$, and $d\operatorname{vol}_{\widetilde g}=d\operatorname{vol}_g$.
  - Explanation: The eigenvalue can be made simple by a small volume-preserving change keeping the chosen eigenfunction fixed.
- **claim-open-persistence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-spec-persistence)): Suppose $g\in\mathcal U$ has a simple positive eigenvalue $\lambda$ with real eigenfunction $t$, and, for a fixed sign score and $N>0$, $$\lambda>N,\qquad c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t)>N\sqrt\lambda.$$ There is a smooth-topology neighborhood $\mathcal V\subset\mathcal U$ of $g$ such that every $g'\in\mathcal V$ has a simple eigenvalue $\lambda'>N$ with a real eigenfunction $t'$ satisfying $$c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t')>N\sqrt{\lambda'}.$$ In particular its nodal-measure ratio exceeds $N$.
  - Explanation: A simple eigenpair with a strict score inequality persists with the same inequality on a smooth neighborhood of metrics.
- **claim-arbitrarily-close-step** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-one-step)): Let $h\in\mathcal U$ and suppose $h-g_0$ has compact support in $P$. For every smooth-topology neighborhood $\mathcal O$ of $h$, every $N>0$, and every integer $k_{\min}$, there are an integer $k\ge k_{\min}$, a metric $\widetilde h\in\mathcal O\cap\mathcal U$, and a real eigenfunction $t$ such that $$-\Delta_{\widetilde h}t=\Lambda_k t,\qquad \Lambda_k>N,\qquad c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t)>N\sqrt{\Lambda_k}$$ for a sign score $\mathcal S$, and $\Lambda_k$ is simple. Both $\widetilde h-g_0$ and $t-u_k^0$ have compact support in $P$. The inequalities persist in a neighborhood of $\widetilde h$ in the sense of Lemma <a href="#spec:persistence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:persistence">40</a>.
  - Explanation: Arbitrarily near any admissible metric there is a metric with a simple eigenvalue whose score exceeds a given level, which persists on a neighborhood.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-radial-corrugation",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\delta>0$ and $g_0>0$, depending only on the fixed function $F$, with the following property. Let $\\psi$ be full with nonvanishing gradient on a neighborhood of the closure of a relatively compact coordinate patch $P$. Suppose that $y=(y_1,y_2)$ is a smooth submersion there, with $$\\,\\mathrm dy(a_\\psi)=0,\n \\qquad\\text{both nonzero singular values of }\\,\\mathrm dy\n       \\text{ in }[1/2,2].$$ Let $A>0$ be constant and satisfy $1/2\\le A/|a_\\psi|\\le2$, and let $0\\le\\chi\\le1$ belong to $C_c^\\infty(P)$. Set $$\\Phi_N=\\psi+\\frac{\\delta A}{N}\\chi F(Ny).$$ For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the function $\\Phi_N$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\\overline P$. Moreover, $\\|\\Phi_N-\\psi\\|_{C^0(P)}\\to0$. Given any $\\epsilon_r>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n |a_{\\Phi_N}|&\\ge(1-\\epsilon_r)|a_\\psi|\n                      &&\\text{on }\\overline P,\\label{eq:radial-no-loss}\\\\\n |a_{\\Phi_N}|&\\ge(1+g_0-\\epsilon_r)|a_\\psi|\n                      &&\\text{where }\\chi=1\\text{ and }Ny\\in E_F.\n                      \\label{eq:radial-gain}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The frequency thresholds may depend on all the fixed patch data and on $\\epsilon_r$; the constants $\\delta,g_0$ do not.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-radial-corrugation",

Set $$\\Phi_N=\\psi+\\frac{\\delta A}{N}\\chi F(Ny).$$ For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the function $\\Phi_N$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\\overline P$. Moreover, $\\|\\Phi_N-\\psi\\|_{C^0(P)}\\to0$. Given any $\\epsilon_r>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n |a_{\\Phi_N}|&\\ge(1-\\epsilon_r)|a_\\psi|\n                      &&\\text{on }\\overline P,\\label{eq:radial-no-loss}\\\\\n |a_{\\Phi_N}|&\\ge(1+g_0-\\epsilon_r)|a_\\psi|\n                      &&\\text{where }\\chi=1\\text{ and }Ny\\in E_F.\n                      \\label{eq:radial-gain}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The frequency thresholds may depend on all the fixed patch data and on $\\epsilon_r$; the constants $\\delta,g_0$ do not.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-spherical-profiles",
        "exact_quote": "There is a $C^2$ neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of $g_*$ such that the following holds. For every smooth $g\\in\\mathcal U$, every $1/5\\le t\\le3/10$, and every $T,\\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth real function $\\phi$ on $\\mathbb S^3$ with $$\\phi_0=\\log t+r^2-t^2,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}(\\phi-\\phi_0)\\Subset\\Omega,\n \\qquad \\|\\phi-\\phi_0\\|_{C^0}<\\varepsilon,$$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\phi$ is strict on $r\\le3/10$;*\n\n2.  *its critical points in this region are exactly $r=0$, and at each such point $H_\\phi$ is positive on a two-plane;*\n\n3.  *$\\phi>\\log r$ for $0<r<t$, while $\\phi<\\log r$ for $t<r\\le1$;*\n\n4.  *$\\displaystyle\\int_\\Omega |a_\\phi|\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g>T$.*\n\n\n*The metric-coordinate comparison constants on $\\Omega$, including the Lipschitz constants of its two-coordinate projections for the ambient Riemannian distance, can be chosen uniformly for $g\\in\\mathcal U$. In particular, $$\\int_\\Omega L_\\phi\\,\\,\\mathrm dx\n \\ge c\\int_\\Omega |a_\\phi|\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g\n \\label{eq:profile-coordinate-mass}$$ with one constant $c>0$ for this neighborhood. Each constructed profile is fixed before a wave frequency is chosen.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-spherical-profiles",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-phase-hessian",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a real symmetric form on a three-dimensional Euclidean space, and let $z=a+ib$, where $a,b$ are real, $a\\perp b$, and $|b|^2=1+|a|^2$. If $a\\ne0$ and $H(a,a)+H(b,b)>0$, there is a complex symmetric form $Q$ such that $$Qz=0,\\qquad \\Re Q<H.\n \\label{eq:phase-hessian-conditions}$$ If $a=0$, the same conclusion follows from $H(b,b)>0$. Moreover, a choice satisfying <a href=\"#eq:phase-hessian-conditions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:phase-hessian-conditions\">[eq:phase-hessian-conditions]</a> continues with locally bounded coefficients and a positive real-part gap under small changes of $H,z$ with $z\\cdot z=-1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-phase-hessian",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a real symmetric form on a three-dimensional Euclidean space, and let $z=a+ib$, where $a,b$ are real, $a\\perp b$, and $|b|^2=1+|a|^2$. If $a\\ne0$ and $H(a,a)+H(b,b)>0$, there is a complex symmetric form $Q$ such that $$Qz=0,\\qquad \\Re Q<H.\n \\label{eq:phase-hessian-conditions}$$ If $a=0$, the same conclusion follows from $H(b,b)>0$. Moreover, a choice satisfying <a href=\"#eq:phase-hessian-conditions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:phase-hessian-conditions\">[eq:phase-hessian-conditions]</a> continues with locally bounded coefficients and a positive real-part gap under small changes of $H,z$ with $z\\cdot z=-1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $H$ be a real symmetric form on a three-dimensional Euclidean space, and let $z=a+ib$, where $a,b$ are real, $a\\perp b$, and $|b|^2=1+|a|^2$. If $a\\ne0$ and $H(a,a)+H(b,b)>0$, there is a complex symmetric form $Q$ such that $$Qz=0,\\qquad \\Re Q<H.\n \\label{eq:phase-hessian-conditions}$$ If $a=0$, the same conclusion follows from $H(b,b)>0$. Moreover, a choice satisfying <a href=\"#eq:phase-hessian-conditions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:phase-hessian-conditions\">[eq:phase-hessian-conditions]</a> continues with locally bounded coefficients and a positive real-part gap under small changes of $H,z$ with $z\\cdot z=-1$.",
      "explanation": "Given the strictness inequality there is a complex symmetric form annihilating the complex phase gradient whose real part lies strictly below the real Hessian, with continuation under small changes.",

      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-waves",
        "exact_quote": "For every pair of nonnegative integers $m,D$, there are common normal coordinate balls about the points of $K_1$ and three waves at every center $p\\in K_1$, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, with the following properties. Each wave is a smooth complex function on the sphere supported in $r<t_2$ and has the form $$Z=\\zeta e^{nS}V,\n \\qquad V=\\sum_{j=0}^J n^{-j}V_j,\n \\label{eq:finite-wave-form}$$ in its center’s normal coordinates. Here $S,V_j$ are complex polynomials and $\\zeta$ is a smooth cutoff equal to one on a common smaller ball. With $\\delta=n^{-1/2}$, the three phases have initial data $$S(p)=\\phi(p),\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}S(p)=z_\\ell,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}S(p)=Q_\\ell,$$ as in <a href=\"#eq:three-phase-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:three-phase-data\">[eq:three-phase-data]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:uniform-phase-gap\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:uniform-phase-gap\">[eq:uniform-phase-gap]</a>. Their amplitudes satisfy $V_0(p)=1$ and $V_j(p)=0$ for $j>0$. On their supports, $$\\Re S(x)-\\phi(x)\n \\le n^{-1/2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)-c\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)^2,\n \\label{eq:wave-localization}$$ with a common $c>0$. Setting $\\Lambda_n=n(n+2)$, one has $$\\bigl|\\nabla^j(\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)Z(x)\\bigr|_g\n       \\le Cn^{-D}e^{n\\phi(x)},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le m.\n \\label{eq:wave-individual-residual}$$ For any further fixed finite collection of derivative orders, the construction can also be required to satisfy $$|\\nabla^jZ(x)|_g\\le C_j n^j e^{n\\phi(x)}\n \\label{eq:wave-individual-derivatives}$$ at those orders. All the constants, polynomial degrees, and ball radii are fixed before $n$; they may depend on $g,\\phi,m,D$ and the further derivative orders.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-waves",
          "exact_quote": "For every pair of nonnegative integers $m,D$, there are common normal coordinate balls about the points of $K_1$ and three waves at every center $p\\in K_1$, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, with the following properties. Here $S,V_j$ are complex polynomials and $\\zeta$ is a smooth cutoff equal to one on a common smaller ball. With $\\delta=n^{-1/2}$, the three phases have initial data $$S(p)=\\phi(p),\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}S(p)=z_\\ell,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}S(p)=Q_\\ell,$$ as in <a href=\"#eq:three-phase-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:three-phase-data\">[eq:three-phase-data]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:uniform-phase-gap\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:uniform-phase-gap\">[eq:uniform-phase-gap]</a>. Their amplitudes satisfy $V_0(p)=1$ and $V_j(p)=0$ for $j>0$. On their supports, $$\\Re S(x)-\\phi(x)\n \\le n^{-1/2}\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)-c\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_g(p,x)^2,\n \\label{eq:wave-localization}$$ with a common $c>0$. Setting $\\Lambda_n=n(n+2)$, one has $$\\bigl|\\nabla^j(\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)Z(x)\\bigr|_g\n       \\le Cn^{-D}e^{n\\phi(x)},\\qquad 0\\le j\\le m.\n \\label{eq:wave-individual-residual}$$ For any further fixed finite collection of derivative orders, the construction can also be required to satisfy $$|\\nabla^jZ(x)|_g\\le C_j n^j e^{n\\phi(x)}\n \\label{eq:wave-individual-derivatives}$$ at those orders. All the constants, polynomial degrees, and ball radii are fixed before $n$; they may depend on $g,\\phi,m,D$ and the further derivative orders.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every pair of nonnegative integers $m,D$, there are common normal coordinate balls about the points of $K_1$ and three waves at every center $p\\in K_1$, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, with the following properties.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sign-area",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:sign-functional\" label=\"prop:sign-functional\"></span> The functional $\\mathcal F_n$ is lower semicontinuous for uniform convergence of real functions. It is unchanged by multiplication by a nonzero real constant or by an everywhere positive continuous function. There is a constant $C_{\\rm area}$, depending only on the fixed coordinates and metric neighborhood, such that for every continuous real $f$ and every $h\\in\\mathcal G$, $$\\mathcal F_n(f)\\le\n       C_{\\rm area}\\mathcal H_h^2(\\{f=0\\}).\n \\label{eq:sign-area-bound}$$ In particular $C_{\\rm area}$ is independent of $\\phi,\\mathscr P$, and $n$. There is also a constant $C_{\\rm sign}$ with the same independence such that $$0\\le\\mathcal F_n(f)\\le\n       C_{\\rm sign}n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx.\n \\label{eq:sign-deterministic-upper}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sign-area",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:sign-functional\" label=\"prop:sign-functional\"></span> The functional $\\mathcal F_n$ is lower semicontinuous for uniform convergence of real functions. It is unchanged by multiplication by a nonzero real constant or by an everywhere positive continuous function. There is a constant $C_{\\rm area}$, depending only on the fixed coordinates and metric neighborhood, such that for every continuous real $f$ and every $h\\in\\mathcal G$, $$\\mathcal F_n(f)\\le\n       C_{\\rm area}\\mathcal H_h^2(\\{f=0\\}).\n \\label{eq:sign-area-bound}$$ In particular $C_{\\rm area}$ is independent of $\\phi,\\mathscr P$, and $n$. There is also a constant $C_{\\rm sign}$ with the same independence such that $$0\\le\\mathcal F_n(f)\\le\n       C_{\\rm sign}n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx.\n \\label{eq:sign-deterministic-upper}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"prop:sign-functional\" label=\"prop:sign-functional\"></span> The functional $\\mathcal F_n$ is lower semicontinuous for uniform convergence of real functions. There is also a constant $C_{\\rm sign}$ with the same independence such that $$0\\le\\mathcal F_n(f)\\le\n       C_{\\rm sign}n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx.\n \\label{eq:sign-deterministic-upper}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-wave-noncancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix any nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. Construct the waves with those residual requirements and with all derivative bounds needed through order $\\max(m_*,2)$. There is a fixed exponent $B=110$, independent of the profile and these orders, such that with probability tending to one as $n\\to\\infty$, $$|U_n|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U_n|_g\\ge n^{-B}W_n\n       \\quad\\hbox{everywhere on the sphere},\n \\label{eq:wave-jet-lower}$$ and simultaneously $$\\begin{split}\n |\\nabla^jU_n|_g&\\le C_j n^{j+4}W_n,\\\\\n |\\nabla^j(\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)U_n|_g\n      &\\le C n^{4-D_*}W_n,\n                 \\qquad 0\\le j\\le m_*.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:wave-global-estimates}$$ The constants and the rate of convergence of the probability may depend on the fixed metric, profile, and orders, but not on $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-wave-sign-expectation",
        "exact_quote": "With a fixed sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ as above, there is a constant $c_{\\rm sign}>0$, independent of the fixed profile and of the wave accuracy orders, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\mathbb E\\mathcal F_n(U_n)\n       \\ge c_{\\rm sign} n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx.\n \\label{eq:sign-expectation-lower}$$ The threshold for $n$ may depend on the entire fixed construction.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-wave-sign-expectation",
          "exact_quote": "With a fixed sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ as above, there is a constant $c_{\\rm sign}>0$, independent of the fixed profile and of the wave accuracy orders, such that for all sufficiently large $n$, $$\\mathbb E\\mathcal F_n(U_n)\n       \\ge c_{\\rm sign} n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx.\n \\label{eq:sign-expectation-lower}$$ The threshold for $n$ may depend on the entire fixed construction.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-wave-realization",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:wave-superposition\" label=\"prop:wave-superposition\"></span> Under the hypotheses of this section, fix arbitrary nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. For every sufficiently large integer $n$ there is a smooth real $U_n$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:wave-jet-lower\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:wave-jet-lower\">[eq:wave-jet-lower]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:wave-global-estimates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:wave-global-estimates\">[eq:wave-global-estimates]</a>, with $B=110$, and $$\\mathcal F_n(U_n)\\ge\n       c_{\\rm wave} n\\int_\\Omega L(x)\\,dx,\n \\label{eq:wave-sign-lower}$$ where $c_{\\rm wave}>0$ depends only on the fixed coordinates and metric neighborhood. Moreover, $$U_n=w_n\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad g=g_*\n       \\qquad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\}.\n \\label{eq:wave-clean-exterior}$$ The thresholds and constants in the differential estimates may depend on the fixed profile and accuracy orders; $B$ and $c_{\\rm wave}$ do not. The sign functional is finite, fixed, and has all the persistence and area-comparison properties of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:sign-functional\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:sign-functional\">[prop:sign-functional]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-wave-realization",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:wave-superposition\" label=\"prop:wave-superposition\"></span> Under the hypotheses of this section, fix arbitrary nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. Moreover, $$U_n=w_n\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad g=g_*\n       \\qquad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\}.\n \\label{eq:wave-clean-exterior}$$ The thresholds and constants in the differential estimates may depend on the fixed profile and accuracy orders; $B$ and $c_{\\rm wave}$ do not. The sign functional is finite, fixed, and has all the persistence and area-comparison properties of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:sign-functional\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:sign-functional\">[prop:sign-functional]</a>.",

      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"prop:wave-superposition\" label=\"prop:wave-superposition\"></span> Under the hypotheses of this section, fix arbitrary nonnegative integers $m_*,D_*$. Moreover, $$U_n=w_n\\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad g=g_*\n       \\qquad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\}.\n \\label{eq:wave-clean-exterior}$$ The thresholds and constants in the differential estimates may depend on the fixed profile and accuracy orders; $B$ and $c_{\\rm wave}$ do not.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-quotients",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth Riemannian manifold. Fix an integer $B\\ge1$, and let $h\\ge0$ and $D_*>0$ be integers. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, a real smooth function $U=U_n$ and a positive continuous function $W=W_n$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n |U|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U|&\\ge n^{-B}W,\n \\label{eq:exact-jet-hypothesis}\\\\\n |\\nabla^jU|&\\le C_j n^{j+4}W,\n &|\\nabla^j\\mathcal R|&\\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W\n \\quad (0\\le j\\le h+2),\n \\label{eq:exact-weighted-hypothesis}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\mathcal R=(\\Delta_g+\\Lambda)U$ and the constants $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal D&=n^2U^2+|\\nabla U|^2,\\qquad\n b=1-\\frac{\\mathcal R U}{\\mathcal D},\\\\\n s&=1-\\Lambda^{-1}\\left[\n \\frac{\\mathcal R n^2U}{\\mathcal D}\n -\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g\\left(\\frac{\\mathcal R}{\\mathcal D}\\nabla U\\right)\\right].\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:exact-scalar-correction}$$ Then $b,s$ are smooth, and, with $\\Delta_b f=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g(b\\nabla f)$, $$\\Delta_bU+\\Lambda sU=0.\n \\label{eq:exact-weighted-equation}$$ They equal one on every open set where $\\mathcal R=0$. Moreover, if $$E=2B+20,\\qquad Q_h=(E+1)(h+2)+10,$$ then $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^h}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^h}\n \\le C_h n^{-D_*+Q_h}.\n \\label{eq:exact-quotient-bound}$$ In particular, for any prescribed integer $P_1\\ge1$, choosing $D_*\\ge P_1+Q_h+2$ makes the left side at most $n^{-P_1}$ for all sufficiently large $n$. In this case $b$ and $s$ are positive for all sufficiently large $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-quotients",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth Riemannian manifold. Fix an integer $B\\ge1$, and let $h\\ge0$ and $D_*>0$ be integers. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal D&=n^2U^2+|\\nabla U|^2,\\qquad\n b=1-\\frac{\\mathcal R U}{\\mathcal D},\\\\\n s&=1-\\Lambda^{-1}\\left[\n \\frac{\\mathcal R n^2U}{\\mathcal D}\n -\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g\\left(\\frac{\\mathcal R}{\\mathcal D}\\nabla U\\right)\\right].\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:exact-scalar-correction}$$ Then $b,s$ are smooth, and, with $\\Delta_b f=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g(b\\nabla f)$, $$\\Delta_bU+\\Lambda sU=0.\n \\label{eq:exact-weighted-equation}$$ They equal one on every open set where $\\mathcal R=0$. Moreover, if $$E=2B+20,\\qquad Q_h=(E+1)(h+2)+10,$$ then $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^h}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^h}\n \\le C_h n^{-D_*+Q_h}.\n \\label{eq:exact-quotient-bound}$$ In particular, for any prescribed integer $P_1\\ge1$, choosing $D_*\\ge P_1+Q_h+2$ makes the left side at most $n^{-P_1}$ for all sufficiently large $n$. In this case $b$ and $s$ are positive for all sufficiently large $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth Riemannian manifold. Fix an integer $B\\ge1$, and let $h\\ge0$ and $D_*>0$ be integers. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, a real smooth function $U=U_n$ and a positive continuous function $W=W_n$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n |U|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U|&\\ge n^{-B}W,\n \\label{eq:exact-jet-hypothesis}\\\\\n |\\nabla^jU|&\\le C_j n^{j+4}W,\n &|\\nabla^j\\mathcal R|&\\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W\n \\quad (0\\le j\\le h+2),\n \\label{eq:exact-weighted-hypothesis}\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $\\mathcal R=(\\Delta_g+\\Lambda)U$ and the constants $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal D&=n^2U^2+|\\nabla U|^2,\\qquad\n b=1-\\frac{\\mathcal R U}{\\mathcal D},\\\\\n s&=1-\\Lambda^{-1}\\left[\n \\frac{\\mathcal R n^2U}{\\mathcal D}\n -\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g\\left(\\frac{\\mathcal R}{\\mathcal D}\\nabla U\\right)\\right].\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:exact-scalar-correction}$$ Then $b,s$ are smooth, and, with $\\Delta_b f=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g(b\\nabla f)$, $$\\Delta_bU+\\Lambda sU=0.\n \\label{eq:exact-weighted-equation}$$ They equal one on every open set where $\\mathcal R=0$. Moreover, if $$E=2B+20,\\qquad Q_h=(E+1)(h+2)+10,$$ then $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^h}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^h}\n \\le C_h n^{-D_*+Q_h}.\n \\label{eq:exact-quotient-bound}$$ In particular, for any prescribed integer $P_1\\ge1$, choosing $D_*\\ge P_1+Q_h+2$ makes the left side at most $n^{-P_1}$ for all sufficiently large $n$. In this case $b$ and $s$ are positive for all sufficiently large $n$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-metric-conductivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold and let $A$ be a smooth positive $g$-self-adjoint endomorphism of $TM$. Put $d=\\det A$, with determinant taken in any $g$-orthonormal frame, and define $$g_A(X,Y)=g(dA^{-1}X,Y).\n \\label{eq:exact-metric-realization}$$ Then $g_A$ is a smooth Riemannian metric and $$\\,\\mathrm dV_{g_A}=d\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g,\\qquad\n \\Delta_{g_A}f=d^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g(A\\nabla f).\n \\label{eq:exact-metric-operator}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-metric-conductivity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold and let $A$ be a smooth positive $g$-self-adjoint endomorphism of $TM$.           "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(M,g)$ be a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold and let $A$ be a smooth positive $g$-self-adjoint endomorphism of $TM$. Put $d=\\det A$, with determinant taken in any $g$-orthonormal frame, and define $$g_A(X,Y)=g(dA^{-1}X,Y).\n \\label{eq:exact-metric-realization}$$ Then $g_A$ is a smooth Riemannian metric and $$\\,\\mathrm dV_{g_A}=d\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g,\\qquad\n \\Delta_{g_A}f=d^{-1}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_g(A\\nabla f).\n \\label{eq:exact-metric-operator}$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-positive-factor",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Given integers $k_2\\ge0$ and $P_2\\ge1$, choose an even integer $l\\ge4$ with $l>k_2+3/2$, and set $$k_1=l,\\qquad P_1=P_2+8.$$ Suppose that smooth functions $b=b_n$ and $s=s_n$ satisfy $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}\n \\le n^{-P_1}.\n \\label{eq:exact-coefficient-smallness}$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, there is a positive smooth function $v=v_n$ solving $$-\\Delta_bv+\\Lambda b^3v^5-\\Lambda sv=0\n \\label{eq:exact-semilinear}$$ and satisfying $$\\|v-1\\|_{C^{k_2}}\\le n^{-P_2}.\n \\label{eq:exact-factor-bound}$$ The required threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed manifold and the displayed finite orders.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-positive-factor",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Given integers $k_2\\ge0$ and $P_2\\ge1$, choose an even integer $l\\ge4$ with $l>k_2+3/2$, and set $$k_1=l,\\qquad P_1=P_2+8.$$ Suppose that smooth functions $b=b_n$ and $s=s_n$ satisfy $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}\n \\le n^{-P_1}.\n \\label{eq:exact-coefficient-smallness}$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, there is a positive smooth function $v=v_n$ solving $$-\\Delta_bv+\\Lambda b^3v^5-\\Lambda sv=0\n \\label{eq:exact-semilinear}$$ and satisfying $$\\|v-1\\|_{C^{k_2}}\\le n^{-P_2}.\n \\label{eq:exact-factor-bound}$$ The required threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed manifold and the displayed finite orders.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $(M,g)$ be a fixed closed smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Given integers $k_2\\ge0$ and $P_2\\ge1$, choose an even integer $l\\ge4$ with $l>k_2+3/2$, and set $$k_1=l,\\qquad P_1=P_2+8.$$ Suppose that smooth functions $b=b_n$ and $s=s_n$ satisfy $$\\|b-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}+\\|s-1\\|_{C^{k_1}}\n \\le n^{-P_1}.\n \\label{eq:exact-coefficient-smallness}$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, there is a positive smooth function $v=v_n$ solving $$-\\Delta_bv+\\Lambda b^3v^5-\\Lambda sv=0\n \\label{eq:exact-semilinear}$$ and satisfying $$\\|v-1\\|_{C^{k_2}}\\le n^{-P_2}.\n \\label{eq:exact-factor-bound}$$ The required threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed manifold and the displayed finite orders.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exactification",
        "exact_quote": "Let $g$ be a fixed smooth metric on $\\mathbb S^3$, and fix $0<t_2<t_3<1$. Assume that $g=g_*$ on $\\{r\\ge t_2\\}$, where $g_*$ is the unit round metric and $r=(x_1^2+x_2^2)^{1/2}$. Fix an integer $B\\ge1$.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every pair of integers $k\\ge0$, $P\\ge1$, there exist finite integers $m_*,D_*$, depending only on $k,P,B$, with the following property. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, there are real smooth functions $U_n$ and positive continuous functions $W_n$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n |U_n|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U_n|\\ge n^{-B}W_n,\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-lower}\\\\\n |\\nabla^jU_n|\\le C_jn^{j+4}W_n,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j((\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)U_n)|\n \\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W_n\n \\quad(0\\le j\\le m_*),\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-upper}\\\\\n U_n=w_n=\\Re(x_1+ix_2)^n\\quad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\},\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-exterior}\n\\end{gathered}$$ where all $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, there are a smooth Riemannian metric $\\widehat g_n$ and a positive smooth function $\\widetilde v_n$ on $\\mathbb S^3$ such that, with $u_n=U_n/\\widetilde v_n$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n (\\Delta_{\\widehat g_n}+\\Lambda_n)u_n=0,\n \\qquad \\|\\widehat g_n-g\\|_{C^k}\\le n^{-P},\n \\label{eq:exact-final-equation}\\\\\n \\widehat g_n=g_*,\\quad \\widetilde v_n=1,\\quad u_n=w_n\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_3\\}.\n \\label{eq:exact-final-exterior}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular $u_n$ is nonzero and has exactly the same pointwise signs and zero set as $U_n$. The threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed input data and on the constants in <a href=\"#eq:exact-proposition-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-proposition-upper\">[eq:exact-proposition-upper]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exactification",
          "exact_quote": "Let $g$ be a fixed smooth metric on $\\mathbb S^3$, and fix $0<t_2<t_3<1$. Assume that $g=g_*$ on $\\{r\\ge t_2\\}$, where $g_*$ is the unit round metric and $r=(x_1^2+x_2^2)^{1/2}$. Fix an integer $B\\ge1$.*\n\n\n\n\n*For every pair of integers $k\\ge0$, $P\\ge1$, there exist finite integers $m_*,D_*$, depending only on $k,P,B$, with the following property. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, there are real smooth functions $U_n$ and positive continuous functions $W_n$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n |U_n|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U_n|\\ge n^{-B}W_n,\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-lower}\\\\\n |\\nabla^jU_n|\\le C_jn^{j+4}W_n,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j((\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)U_n)|\n \\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W_n\n \\quad(0\\le j\\le m_*),\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-upper}\\\\\n U_n=w_n=\\Re(x_1+ix_2)^n\\quad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\},\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-exterior}\n\\end{gathered}$$ where all $C_j$ are independent of $n$. Then, for all sufficiently large $n$, there are a smooth Riemannian metric $\\widehat g_n$ and a positive smooth function $\\widetilde v_n$ on $\\mathbb S^3$ such that, with $u_n=U_n/\\widetilde v_n$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n (\\Delta_{\\widehat g_n}+\\Lambda_n)u_n=0,\n \\qquad \\|\\widehat g_n-g\\|_{C^k}\\le n^{-P},\n \\label{eq:exact-final-equation}\\\\\n \\widehat g_n=g_*,\\quad \\widetilde v_n=1,\\quad u_n=w_n\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_3\\}.\n \\label{eq:exact-final-exterior}\n\\end{gathered}$$ In particular $u_n$ is nonzero and has exactly the same pointwise signs and zero set as $U_n$. The threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed input data and on the constants in <a href=\"#eq:exact-proposition-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-proposition-upper\">[eq:exact-proposition-upper]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }

Suppose that, for all sufficiently large integers $n$, there are real smooth functions $U_n$ and positive continuous functions $W_n$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n |U_n|+n^{-1}|\\nabla U_n|\\ge n^{-B}W_n,\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-lower}\\\\\n |\\nabla^jU_n|\\le C_jn^{j+4}W_n,\\qquad\n |\\nabla^j((\\Delta_g+\\Lambda_n)U_n)|\n \\le C_jn^{4-D_*}W_n\n \\quad(0\\le j\\le m_*),\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-upper}\\\\\n U_n=w_n=\\Re(x_1+ix_2)^n\\quad\\hbox{on }\\{r\\ge t_2\\},\n \\label{eq:exact-proposition-exterior}\n\\end{gathered}$$ where all $C_j$ are independent of $n$. The threshold for $n$ may depend on the fixed input data and on the constants in <a href=\"#eq:exact-proposition-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exact-proposition-upper\">[eq:exact-proposition-upper]</a>.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pinned-simplicity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h$ be a smooth metric on $\\mathbb S^3$ and let $u$ be a nonzero real smooth function satisfying $$-\\Delta_hu=\\Lambda_nu,\\qquad \\Lambda_n=n(n+2),\\qquad n\\ge2.$$ Suppose that, on an annulus $\\mathcal A=\\{a<r<b\\}$ with $0<a<b<1$, one has $h=g_*$ and $u=w_n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-simple-eigenpair-continuity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a closed smooth manifold, let $h_0$ be a smooth Riemannian metric, and suppose that $\\lambda_0>0$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\\Delta_{h_0}$ with real eigenfunction $u_0$ normalized by $\\|u_0\\|_{L^2(h_0)}=1$. For every open interval $I$ containing $\\lambda_0$, there is a $C^\\infty$ neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of $h_0$ and, for every $h\\in\\mathcal U$, a simple eigenvalue $\\lambda(h)\\in I$ with a normalized real eigenfunction $u(h)$. One can choose the signs so that $$\\lambda(h)\\longrightarrow\\lambda_0,\n \\qquad u(h)\\longrightarrow u_0\\quad\\hbox{in }C^\\infty(M)\n \\quad\\hbox{as }h\\longrightarrow h_0.$$ In particular, if $\\mathcal F$ is lower semicontinuous on $C^0(M)$, invariant under nonzero real rescaling, and $\\mathcal F(u_0)>T$, then $\\mathcal U$ can be made small enough that $\\mathcal F(u(h))>T$ for all $h\\in\\mathcal U$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-simple-eigenpair-continuity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a closed smooth manifold, let $h_0$ be a smooth Riemannian metric, and suppose that $\\lambda_0>0$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\\Delta_{h_0}$ with real eigenfunction $u_0$ normalized by $\\|u_0\\|_{L^2(h_0)}=1$. For every open interval $I$ containing $\\lambda_0$, there is a $C^\\infty$ neighborhood $\\mathcal U$ of $h_0$ and, for every $h\\in\\mathcal U$, a simple eigenvalue $\\lambda(h)\\in I$ with a normalized real eigenfunction $u(h)$. One can choose the signs so that $$\\lambda(h)\\longrightarrow\\lambda_0,\n \\qquad u(h)\\longrightarrow u_0\\quad\\hbox{in }C^\\infty(M)\n \\quad\\hbox{as }h\\longrightarrow h_0.$$ In particular, if $\\mathcal F$ is lower semicontinuous on $C^0(M)$, invariant under nonzero real rescaling, and $\\mathcal F(u_0)>T$, then $\\mathcal U$ can be made small enough that $\\mathcal F(u(h))>T$ for all $h\\in\\mathcal U$.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $M$ be a closed smooth manifold, let $h_0$ be a smooth Riemannian metric, and suppose that $\\lambda_0>0$ is a simple eigenvalue of $-\\Delta_{h_0}$ with real eigenfunction $u_0$ normalized by $\\|u_0\\|_{L^2(h_0)}=1$. One can choose the signs so that $$\\lambda(h)\\longrightarrow\\lambda_0,\n \\qquad u(h)\\longrightarrow u_0\\quad\\hbox{in }C^\\infty(M)\n \\quad\\hbox{as }h\\longrightarrow h_0.$$ In particular, if $\\mathcal F$ is lower semicontinuous on $C^0(M)$, invariant under nonzero real rescaling, and $\\mathcal F(u_0)>T$, then $\\mathcal U$ can be made small enough that $\\mathcal F(u(h))>T$ for all $h\\in\\mathcal U$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-one-step",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $g\\in\\mathcal V$ agrees with $g_*$ on $r\\ge R_0$, where $$\\frac15\\le R_0<R_1\\le\\frac3{10}.$$ Let $\\mathcal N$ be any $C^\\infty$ neighborhood of $g$, let $A>0$, and let $N\\ge2$ be an integer. There are an integer $n\\ge N$, a metric $g'\\in\\mathcal N\\cap\\mathcal V$ agreeing with $g_*$ on $r\\ge R_1$, and a nonzero real smooth function $u$ such that $-\\Delta_{g'}u=\\Lambda_nu$ and $\\Lambda_n$ is simple. Moreover, there is an open neighborhood $\\mathcal W$ of $g'$ in $\\mathcal V$ such that every $h\\in\\mathcal W$ has an exact real eigenfunction $u_h$ with eigenvalue $\\lambda_h$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:one-step-open-witness}\n \\Lambda_n-1<\\lambda_h<\\Lambda_n+1,\n \\qquad\n \\frac{\\mathcal H_h^2(\\{u_h=0\\})}{\\sqrt{\\lambda_h}}>A.$$ This neighborhood is obtained by retaining one fixed sign functional and one fixed strict numerical threshold.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "There is $d_*>0$, depending only on the comparison constant $K$ and fixed auxiliary functions, with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-env-gaps",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $h\\in\\mathcal U$ with $\\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\\Subset P$. There are nested open coordinate boxes $$\\overline D\\cup\\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\\subset U_0\n \\Subset U_1\\Subset U_2\\Subset U_3\\Subset U_4\\Subset P.$$ For every $L_*>0$ there is a smooth strict envelope $f$ for $h$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline P$, and a number $\\sigma>0$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n f>f_0+\\sigma\\quad\\hbox{on }\\overline U_0,\n \\qquad\n f<f_0-\\sigma\\quad\\hbox{on }\\overline P\\setminus U_1,\n \\label{env:gap-inequalities}\\\\\n \\int_D\\sqrt{2+|\\nabla_h f|_h^2}\\,dx>L_*.\n \\label{env:large-integral}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The envelope and all these patches are fixed before the frequency $k$ is chosen.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-env-gaps",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $h\\in\\mathcal U$ with $\\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\\Subset P$. There are nested open coordinate boxes $$\\overline D\\cup\\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\\subset U_0\n \\Subset U_1\\Subset U_2\\Subset U_3\\Subset U_4\\Subset P.$$ For every $L_*>0$ there is a smooth strict envelope $f$ for $h$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline P$, and a number $\\sigma>0$, such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n f>f_0+\\sigma\\quad\\hbox{on }\\overline U_0,\n \\qquad\n f<f_0-\\sigma\\quad\\hbox{on }\\overline P\\setminus U_1,\n \\label{env:gap-inequalities}\\\\\n \\int_D\\sqrt{2+|\\nabla_h f|_h^2}\\,dx>L_*.\n \\label{env:large-integral}\n\\end{gathered}$$ The envelope and all these patches are fixed before the frequency $k$ is chosen.",
          "source_id": "PM-350.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $h\\in\\mathcal U$ with $\\operatorname{supp}(h-g_0)\\Subset P$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-wave-packets",
        "exact_quote": "For any integers $\\ell,J\\ge0$, and all sufficiently large integers $k$, there are packets indexed by $$Y_k=(k^{-1}\\mathbb Z^4)\\cap U_3,\\qquad y\\in Y_k,\\quad 1\\le j\\le3,$$ of the form $$p_{yj,k}(x)=e^{k\\Phi_{yj}(x)}A_{yj,k}(x)\\xi(x-y).\n \\label{wave:packet-form}$$ Here $\\xi$ is a smooth cutoff supported in a fixed small ball, equal to one in a smaller ball; all translated supporting balls lie in $P$. The phases are complex polynomials in $x-y$, and the amplitudes are polynomials of fixed finite degree in $x-y$ and $k^{-1}$. Their coefficients are uniformly bounded over the centers and indices. They satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\Phi_{yj}(y)&=f(y),&\n d\\Phi_{yj}(y)&=df(y)+i b_j^\\flat,\\notag\\\\\n \\Re\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_h\\Phi_{yj}(y)&=H_y-c_{yj}h_y,&\n A_{yj,k}(y)&=1.\n \\label{wave:packet-jets}\n\\end{aligned}$$ On each supporting ball, for positive constants $c,C$, $$-C|x-y|^2\\le\\Re\\Phi_{yj}(x)-f(x)\n       \\le-c|x-y|^2,\n \\qquad\n A_{yj,k}(x)=1+O(|x-y|+k^{-1}).\n \\label{wave:gaussian-bounds}$$ The amplitude and each of its fixed-order derivatives are uniformly bounded. For every fixed multi-index $\\alpha$, $$|\\partial^\\alpha p_{yj,k}(x)|\n \\le C_\\alpha k^{|\\alpha|}\n              e^{kf(x)-ck|x-y|^2},\n \\label{wave:packet-derivatives}$$ after decreasing $c$ if necessary, and $$\\sup_{x\\in P}e^{-kf(x)}\n \\sum_{|\\alpha|\\le\\ell}\n \\big|\\partial^\\alpha(\\Delta_h+\\Lambda_k)p_{yj,k}(x)\\big|\n \\le C_{\\ell,J}k^{-J}.\n \\label{wave:packet-residual}$$ The radii, coefficients, constants, and frequency threshold are fixed before $k$ varies.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For any predetermined integers $\\ell,J\\ge0$, choose the packets of Proposition <a href=\"#wave:packets\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"wave:packets\">28</a> with derivative order $\\ell$ and residual power $J+6$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-corr-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the background $M,g_0,P,u_k^0,\\Lambda_k$ of Lemma <a href=\"#env:background-mode\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"env:background-mode\">25</a>, patches $U_2\\Subset U_3\\Subset U_4\\Subset P$, a smooth metric $h$ such that $h-g_0$ has compact support in $U_2$, and a fixed smooth function $f$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline P$. Put $$W=e^{kf}+e^{kf_0}.$$ For every integer $p\\ge0$ and every $L>0$, there are finite integers $\\ell,J$ with the following property. Suppose smooth real functions $u=u_k$, for arbitrarily large integers $k$, satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n &\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}(u-u_k^0)\\Subset U_2,\n \\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}R\\Subset U_2,\n \\qquad R=(\\Delta_h+\\Lambda_k)u, \\label{corr:support}\\tag{C1}\\\\\n &W^{-1}|\\partial^\\alpha u|\n       \\le C_\\alpha k^{|\\alpha|+5}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }P,\\quad |\\alpha|\\le\\ell+1,\n       \\label{corr:derivatives}\\tag{C2}\\\\\n &\\bigl(u^2+k^{-2}|du|_{\\mathrm{Euc}}^2\\bigr)^{1/2}\n       \\ge k^{-200}W\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }\\overline U_2,\\label{corr:noncancellation}\\tag{C3}\\\\\n &W^{-1}|\\partial^\\alpha R|\n       \\le C_R k^{-J}\n       \\quad\\hbox{on }P,\\quad |\\alpha|\\le\\ell.\n       \\label{corr:residual}\\tag{C4}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constants in these estimates are independent of $k$; the supports in <a href=\"#corr:support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"corr:support\">[corr:support]</a> need not be independent of $k$. For all sufficiently large such $k$, there are a smooth ordinary metric $g_*$ and a smooth positive function $b_*$ on $M$ such that $$\\|g_*-h\\|_{C^p}=O(k^{-L}),\\qquad\n t=b_*u,\\qquad (\\Delta_{g_*}+\\Lambda_k)t=0.$$ Moreover, $g_*-g_0$ and $t-u_k^0$ have compact support in $U_4$. In particular, the zero set and every strict sign comparison of $u$ are preserved.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-corr-weighted",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#corr:support\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"corr:support\">[corr:support]</a>–<a href=\"#corr:residual\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"corr:residual\">[corr:residual]</a>, there are a smooth symmetric contravariant tensor $F$ and a smooth scalar $\\vartheta$, both compactly supported in $U_2$, such that $$\\label{corr:weighted-equation}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_h\\bigl((h^{-1}+F)\\,du\\bigr)\n       +\\Lambda_k(1+\\vartheta)u=0.$$ For each integer $0\\le r\\le\\ell-1$, their estimates through order $r$ use only derivatives of $u$ through order $r+2$ and of $R$ through order $r+1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-corr-conformal",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the smooth metric $h$ on the closed four-manifold $M$, and an even integer $s>2$.
