        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-corr-localization",
        "exact_quote": "For the data <a href=\"#corr:localized-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"corr:localized-data\">[corr:localized-data]</a>, suppose that $\\widehat Z-1$ is sufficiently small in $C^1$ and $\\rho>0$. Then an ordinary smooth metric $g_*$, equal to $g_0$ outside a compact subset of $U_4$, satisfies $(\\Delta_{g_*}+\\Lambda_k)t=0$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-persistence",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $g\\in\\mathcal U$ has a simple positive eigenvalue $\\lambda$ with real eigenfunction $t$, and, for a fixed sign score and $N>0$, $$\\lambda>N,\\qquad c_{\\mathrm H}\\mathcal S(t)>N\\sqrt\\lambda.$$ There is a smooth-topology neighborhood $\\mathcal V\\subset\\mathcal U$ of $g$ such that every $g'\\in\\mathcal V$ has a simple eigenvalue $\\lambda'>N$ with a real eigenfunction $t'$ satisfying $$c_{\\mathrm H}\\mathcal S(t')>N\\sqrt{\\lambda'}.$$ In particular its nodal-measure ratio exceeds $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-one-step",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h\\in\\mathcal U$ and suppose $h-g_0$ has compact support in $P$. Both $\\widetilde h-g_0$ and $t-u_k^0$ have compact support in $P$. The inequalities persist in a neighborhood of $\\widetilde h$ in the sense of Lemma <a href=\"#spec:persistence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"spec:persistence\">40</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-350.2"
      },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Strict and full profile** ([source](#stmt-def-strict-profile)): A smooth function $\phi$ is *strict* at a point if there exists a real vector $b$ at that point such that $$b\perp a_\phi,\qquad \|b\|=L_\phi,\qquad H_\phi(a_\phi,a_\phi)+H_\phi(b,b)>0. \label{eq:profile-strict}$$ It is *full* at that point if the last inequality holds for every $b$ satisfying the first two conditions.
- **Sign score** ([source](#stmt-score-definition)): Subdivide $D$, up to its grid boundaries, into finitely many disjoint open coordinate cubes $Q$, fine enough that numbers $B_Q>0$ satisfy $$B_Q\le B(x)\le 2B_Q\qquad (x\in Q).$$ Choose $c_0>0$ as in Lemma <a href="#score:expectation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="score:expectation">33</a> below and put $$\ell_Q=\frac{c_0}{kB_Q},\qquad Q^- =\{x\in Q:\operatorname{dist}_{\infty}(x,\partial Q)>\ell_Q\}, \qquad T_k=\sum_Q\ell_Q^{-1}\|Q\|.$$ For a real continuous function $\psi$ on $M$, define $$\label{score:formula} \mathcal S(\psi)=\sum_Q\ell_Q^{-1} \int_{Q^-}\sum_{i=1}^4 \mathbf 1_{\{\psi(x)\psi(x+\ell_Q d_i)<0\}}\,dx,$$ where $d_i$ are the four coordinate unit vectors and $\|Q\|$ denotes coordinate volume. The subdivision and $k$ are part of the score’s definition and remain fixed when $\psi$ varies.
- **Gradient, scale factor and Hessian of a profile** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Write $$a_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g\phi,\qquad L_\phi=(1+\|a_\phi\|^2)^{1/2},\qquad H_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi.$$
- **Preparation set and its fraction** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The periodic set $$E_h=\{t:h''(t)>1\}$$ has fraction $p_h=1/3$ in each period.
- **Radial profile function f** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Define $f(s)=\int_0^s f'(t)\,\,\mathrm dt$.

          "anchor": "pm-h-7",
          "exact_quote": "##### Profiles with large mean gradient A smooth real function $\\phi$ will determine the amplitude envelope $e^{n\\phi}$ at frequency $n$ for the waves in Section 3. Their construction requires a strict Hessian inequality, whereas their eventual nodal-area lower bound grows with the integrated gradient of $\\phi$. We show that these two requirements are compatible: a local perturbation can increase the gradient integral arbitrarily while preserving the Hessian condition and changing $\\phi$ arbitrarily little in $C^0$. We then apply this construction on $\\mathbb S^3$, preserving a prescribed radial profile outside one coordinate cube. Throughout this section, gradients, Hessians, and norms are computed using a fixed smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on a three-dimensional manifold. Write $$a_\\phi=\\mathop{\\mathrm{grad}}_g\\phi,\\qquad L_\\phi=(1+|a_\\phi|^2)^{1/2},\\qquad H_\\phi=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_g\\phi.$$ <div id=\"def:strict-profile\" class=\"definition\"> **Definition 3** (Strict and full profiles). A smooth function $\\phi$ is *strict* at a point if there exists a real vector $b$ at that point such that $$b\\perp a_\\phi,\\qquad |b|=L_\\phi,\\qquad H_\\phi(a_\\phi,a_\\phi)+H_\\phi(b,b)>0. \\label{eq:profile-strict}$$ It is *full* at that point if the last inequality holds for every $b$ satisfying the first two conditions. </div> For a symmetric form $H$ and a unit vector $v\\perp a$, it is convenient to set $$\\mathcal T(H,a;v)=H(a,a)+(1+|a|^2)H(v,v).$$ Fullness on a compact set where $a_\\phi$ does not vanish gives a positive minimum of $\\mathcal T(H_\\phi,a_\\phi;v)$ over the transverse unit-circle bundle. That minimum is allowed to depend on the profile. The main construction in this section is local. <div id=\"prop:profile-amplification\" class=\"proposition\"> **Proposition 4** (Amplification of the mean gradient). *Let $\\Omega$ be a coordinate cube with compact closure in a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Suppose that $\\phi$ is smooth on a neighborhood of $\\overline\\Omega$, is strict there, and satisfies $a_\\phi\\ne0$ there. Given $T>0$ and $\\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth function $\\widehat\\phi$ on the same neighborhood such that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}(\\widehat\\phi-\\phi)\\Subset\\Omega,\\qquad \\|\\widehat\\phi-\\phi\\|_{C^0(\\Omega)}<\\varepsilon, \\qquad \\int_\\Omega |a_{\\widehat\\phi}|\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g>T.$$ The function $\\widehat\\phi$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\\overline\\Omega$.* *More precisely, there is a constant $\\eta>0$, determined only by the fixed periodic functions chosen below, such that one finite cycle of the construction multiplies $\\int_\\Omega|a_\\phi|\\,\\,\\mathrm dV_g$ by at least $1+\\eta$. The cycle can have arbitrarily small $C^0$ size. Its patch sizes and frequency thresholds may depend on the entire incoming profile and metric.* </div> The cycle has two parts. A first perturbation makes the profile full on a fixed positive fraction of its gradient mass while changing the gradient arbitrarily little. A radial perturbation in the two transverse variables then increases the gradient on a fixed fraction of that region. The radial perturbation preserves strictness, allowing the cycle to be repeated.",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-period-fraction | fraction p_h of a period where the second derivative exceeds one | = | $1/3$ | fixed absolute constant |
| q-jet-exponent | fixed exponent in the real-jet lower bound | = | $110$ | independent of profile and orders |
| q-noncancel-power | normalized value-gradient lower bound | ≥ | $k^{-200}$ | independent of requested accuracy |
| q-background-eigenvalue | background eigenvalue on the product manifold | = | $2k^2+k$ | integer k |
| q-sphere-eigenvalue | spherical eigenvalue | = | $n(n+2)$ | integer n at least two |
| q-cycle-fraction | effective mass fraction of one amplification cycle | = | $\frac{p_h p_F}{64}$ | fixed absolute constants |
| q-sign-expectation | expected sign functional | ≥ | $c_{\rm sign} n\int_\Omega L(x)\,dx$ | constant independent of profile |
| q-wave-sign | sign functional of the selected wave | ≥ | $c_{\rm wave} n\int_\Omega L(x)\,dx$ | constant depends only on coordinates and metric neighborhood |
| q-wave-residual | weighted residual of an individual localized wave | ≤ | $Cn^{-D}e^{n\phi(x)}$ | constants depend on metric, profile and orders |
| q-scalar-quotient-bound | distance of conductivity and density from one | ≤ | $C_h n^{-D_*+Q_h}$ | large frequency |
| q-metric-closeness | distance of the exact metric from the given metric | ≤ | $n^{-P}$ | large frequency |
| q-score-upper | sign score | ≤ | $4T_k$ | fixed subdivision and k |
| q-score-large | sign score of the good realization | ≥ | $c_{\mathrm s}k\int_D B(x)\,dx$ | constant independent of envelope and accuracy |
| q-eigenvalue-gap | gap between consecutive spherical eigenvalues | = | $2n+3$ | integer n |
| q-delta-choice | corrugation amplitude constraint | ≤ | $\frac1{8R}$ | independent of the envelope |
| q-expected-score | expected sign score | ≥ | $c_{\mathrm E}T_k$ | constants independent of envelope |
| q-weighted-exponent | sufficient loss exponent for the weighted correction | = | $1000(r+2)$ | independent of residual accuracy |
| q-hausdorff-comparison | nodal Hausdorff three-measure | ≥ | $c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(\psi)$ | constant independent of subdivision, frequency and envelope |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **r-localize** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-localization): finite subcover, grid refinement, Lebesgue density and rectangle approximation -> disjoint cubes with cutoff cores; periodic averaging
- **r-prepare** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-preparation): one-variable oscillation with a Schur complement choice of amplitude -> full region carrying a fixed fraction of gradient mass
- **r-radial** [claim](#stmt-lem-radial-corrugation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-radial-corrugation): radial corrugation in two transverse variables with Hessian trace estimates -> gradient gain on an annular set with strictness kept
- **r-amplify** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile-amplification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-radial-corrugation-2): iterate preparation and radial corrugation cycles -> profile with gradient integral above the target
- **r-spherical** [claim](#stmt-prop-spherical-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-spherical-profiles): explicit round Hessian eigenvalues then amplification inside the cube -> global strict profiles on the sphere
- **r-phase** [claim](#stmt-lem-phase-hessian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-phase-hessian): real and imaginary parts of a symmetric form, compatibility of prescribed columns -> complex phase Hessian below the real one
- **r-finite-waves** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-waves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-waves): finite Taylor construction of eikonal and transport jets with polynomial right inverse -> localized waves with small weighted residual
- **r-sign-area** [claim](#stmt-lem-sign-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sign-area): Fatou lemma and slicing with Lipschitz projections -> sign functional bounded by nodal area
- **r-noncancel** [claim](#stmt-lem-wave-noncancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wave-noncancellation): Gaussian density bound on a net plus Lipschitz interpolation -> value-gradient lower bound with high probability
- **r-sign-expectation** [claim](#stmt-lem-wave-sign-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-wave-sign-expectation): bivariate Gaussian correlation estimate along coordinate segments -> expected sign functional lower bound
- **r-wave-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-wave-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-wave-realization): expectation restricted to the good event and selection of a realization -> one wave with all required estimates
- **r-quotients** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-quotients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-quotients): explicit quotient formulas and weighted derivative bookkeeping -> conductivity and density near one
- **r-conductivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-metric-conductivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-metric-conductivity): determinant computation and energy identity -> metric realizing a conductivity
- **r-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-positive-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-positive-factor): contraction mapping in Sobolev spaces with elliptic bootstrap -> positive conjugating factor
- **r-exactify** [claim](#stmt-prop-exactification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exactification): cutoff of the factor and annular determinant repair by a transverse projection -> exact eigenpair with unchanged exterior
- **r-pencil** [claim](#stmt-lem-nondegenerate-pencil) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-nondegenerate-pencil): maximal rank argument with a determinant expansion -> a nondegenerate form
- **r-pinned** [claim](#stmt-prop-pinned-simplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pinned-simplicity): transverse nonvanishing, trace-free tensor annihilating the gradient, compactness -> simple eigenvalue with the same eigenfunction
- **r-continuity** [claim](#stmt-lem-simple-eigenpair-continuity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simple-eigenpair-continuity): min-max continuity and elliptic compactness -> persistent eigenpair and score bound
- **r-one-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-one-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-one-step): combine profile, wave, exactification, simplicity and persistence -> open witness neighborhood with large nodal ratio
- **r-main-limit** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-one-step-2): nested closed balls in a complete metric on smooth tensors -> single smooth limiting metric
- **r-background** [claim](#stmt-env-background-mode) / [proof](#proof-stmt-env-background-mode): harmonic polynomial restriction and explicit gradient identity -> background eigenfunctions with gradient bound
- **r-amplify-four** [claim](#stmt-env-amplify) / [proof](#proof-stmt-env-amplify): cell-wise corrugation with strictness checked in two regimes -> envelope with larger gradient integral
- **r-gaps** [claim](#stmt-env-gaps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-env-gaps): gap perturbation followed by finitely many amplifications -> envelope with gaps and large integral
- **r-packets** [claim](#stmt-wave-packets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wave-packets): finite Taylor eikonal and transport construction with explicit right inverse -> localized packets
- **r-residual-four** [claim](#stmt-wave-residual) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wave-residual): sum of packet bounds and gap estimates for background and cutoff terms -> weighted residual and derivative bounds
- **r-noncancel-four** [claim](#stmt-wave-noncancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-wave-noncancellation): five independent Gaussian directions, small-ball bound and net -> value-gradient lower bound
- **r-score-measure** [claim](#stmt-score-measure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-score-measure): backward sweep cylinders and covering argument -> nodal measure bounded below by score
- **r-score-expectation** [claim](#stmt-score-expectation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-score-expectation): Gaussian correlation and opposite-sign probability -> expected score lower bound
- **r-score-good** [claim](#stmt-score-good) / [proof](#proof-stmt-score-good): restrict expectation to the good event and select a realization -> realization with all bounds
- **r-corr-weighted** [claim](#stmt-corr-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corr-weighted): explicit tensor and density with cutoffs on the gradient and value -> weighted equation with small corrections
- **r-conformal** [claim](#stmt-corr-conformal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corr-conformal): contraction with coercive linearization and bootstrap -> positive conformal factor
- **r-collar** [claim](#stmt-corr-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corr-localization): projection onto the level-set tangent space and determinant scaling -> ordinary metric with exact eigenfunction
- **r-corr-realization** [claim](#stmt-corr-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-corr-localization-2): explicit order of choices through weighted correction, conformal factor and collar repair -> exact eigenfunction of a nearby ordinary metric
- **r-spec-simple** [claim](#stmt-spec-simple) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-simple): tangential variation, unique continuation, multiplicity reduction -> simple eigenvalue with fixed eigenfunction
- **r-spec-persistence** [claim](#stmt-spec-persistence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-persistence): apply continuity of simple eigenpairs to the score functional -> open persistence neighborhood
- **r-spec-one-step** [claim](#stmt-spec-one-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-one-step): combine envelope, good realization, ordinary-metric realization, simplicity and persistence -> nearby metric with persistent large score
- **r-four-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-one-step-2): nested closed balls in a complete metric on smooth tensors -> single limiting metric with the violating sequence

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-fixed-metric-unbounded-ratio**: For one fixed smooth metric on the three-sphere, chosen in any prescribed smooth neighborhood of the round metric, there is a sequence of exact real eigenfunctions whose nodal measure divided by the square root of the eigenvalue is unbounded.
- **inv-four-dimensional-counterexample**: On the product of a two-sphere and a two-torus there is one smooth metric carrying eigenfunctions whose normalized three-dimensional nodal measure diverges.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-real-analytic-metrics**: Use the counterexample to doubt the square-root upper bound for real-analytic metrics. -> The constructions rely on non-analytic smooth cutoffs and corrugations that analytic metrics do not allow.
- **anti-power-law-claim**: Quote the result as a polynomial excess over the square-root rate in dimensions three or four. -> Only unbounded growth of the normalized measure is proved, with no rate.
- **anti-numerical-synthesis**: Turn the construction into a numerical procedure producing the metric or its eigenfunctions. -> The metric is a limit of nested neighborhoods with frequency thresholds chosen after finite orders, so there is no finite procedure.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements obtained as limits of nested smooth-metric neighborhoods, with frequency thresholds chosen after finite derivative orders and no finite computable output; the paper itself states that all finite orders are chosen before the frequency is sent to infinity.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence of one smooth metric through nested neighbourhoods and limits. It gives no program, API or data format that a software module could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The metrics are produced only as a limit of infinitely many perturbations, and the results are qualitative. They give no simulable scene, level or physics rule, and the paper makes no quantitative growth claim for dimensions three and four. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No decision procedure, checker or certificate-producing routine is stated. The sign score is a proof device whose use depends on fixed subdivisions and frequency thresholds that are only shown to exist. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Although the proofs use explicit-looking steps such as cycles of corrugation, frequency and nested balls, every step quantifies over sufficiently large unspecified thresholds. The result is an existence theorem, so no algorithm with time or space bounds is claimed. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No container, index or representation is proposed. The objects are smooth tensor fields on a closed manifold. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no computation to accelerate: the argument is analytic, with constants and thresholds that exist but are not specified. A hardware mapping would have nothing to target. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns hardness, adversaries or keys. The Gaussian superposition is used for a probabilistic existence argument, not as a source of cryptographic randomness. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, loss or generalisation statement appears. The Gaussian coefficients select one good realisation of a wave, which is a proof device and not a statistical model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective to optimise and no feasibility or approximation guarantee. The metric is obtained as the limit point of nested closed balls, not as a solution of a program. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no notion of nodes, messages, consistency or fault tolerance. Its local corrections on disjoint patches are geometric and do not model communication. |
| other (high-frequency wave and modal analysis (qualitative caution)) | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The result is a warning for modal and wave analysis in media of three or four dimensions with smooth, non-analytic coefficients. The classical square-root growth of zero-set measure cannot be assumed there for every fixed smooth metric, even for metrics arbitrarily close to the round one. No construction or quantitative rate is supplied, so this is only guidance on which hypotheses to check. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-smooth-not-analytic** (hypothesis): The counterexamples concern merely smooth metrics. For real-analytic metrics the sharp two-sided estimate is cited as known, so the refutation must not be transferred to that class. Blocked use: ['Doubting the square-root upper bound for real-analytic metrics']
- **L-dimension-three-four** (hypothesis): The theorems are stated for the three-sphere and for the product of a two-sphere and a two-torus. In dimension two the upper inequality is stated to hold, so the construction gives nothing there. Blocked use: ['Applying the counterexample to closed surfaces', 'Reading the result as a statement about every dimension']
- **L-no-power-law** (formal_scope): Only divergence of the normalized nodal measure along a sequence is proved; no rate and no power-law excess is asserted in dimensions three or four. Blocked use: ['Quoting a polynomial excess over the square-root rate']
- **L-lower-bound-untouched** (formal_scope): The lower-bound half of the conjecture is not contradicted; only the upper inequality fails. Blocked use: ['Claiming the nodal lower bound fails']
- **L-one-metric-per-sequence** (edge_case): Each eigenfunction sequence belongs to one fixed metric. A metric chosen anew at each eigenvalue would not give the statement. Blocked use: ['Using metrics that vary with the eigenvalue as a counterexample to the fixed-metric conjecture']
- **L-nonconstructive-limit** (nonconstructive): The metric is a limit of nested neighborhoods, with finite orders fixed first and frequency thresholds chosen afterwards. No finite procedure, rate or bound on the construction is given. Blocked use: ['Numerical synthesis of the metric', 'Extracting an effective running time or explicit thresholds']
- **L-probabilistic-selection** (nonconstructive): The good wave is selected from a Gaussian superposition by an averaging argument restricted to a high-probability event, so its coefficients are not explicit. Blocked use: ['Treating the wave as an explicitly constructed function', 'Using the Gaussian coefficients as a statistical model']
- **L-sign-score-fixed-data** (approximation): The sign score bounds nodal measure from below only for a fixed subdivision and frequency, up to a comparability constant of the metric neighborhood. Blocked use: ['Using the score as a general nodal-volume estimator with sharp constants']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 350 (`lean/docs/350.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Sharp nodal length on smooth surfaces](../../preprints/Sharp-nodal-length-on-smooth-surfaces-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Smooth counterexamples to Yau's nodal upper bound in dimensions three and four](../../preprints/Smooth-counterexamples-to-Yaus-nodal-upper-bound-in-dimensions-three-and-four-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Power-law violations of Yau's nodal upper bound](../../preprints/Power-law-violations-of-Yaus-nodal-upper-bound-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Yau's nodal-set conjecture predicts nodal size of order $\sqrt\lambda$ for Laplace eigenfunctions of eigenvalue $\lambda$. The formalization proves the upper bound on every fixed smooth closed connected Riemannian surface: there is a surface-dependent constant $C$ such that the one-dimensional Hausdorff measure of the zero set of every nonzero real eigenfunction with $\lambda>0$ is at most $C\sqrt\lambda$. The known lower bound is not part of this selected statement.
>
> The formalized results contradict Yau's proposed $O(\sqrt\lambda)$ upper bound for nodal measure in dimensions three and four. On $S^3$, every prescribed smooth neighborhood of the round metric contains one fixed smooth metric with an eigenfunction sequence of unbounded nodal-measure ratio. A second fixed metric on $S^2\times T^2$ has the same property. In both cases the eigenvalues tend to infinity and the intrinsic nodal measures are finite.
>
> Yau's nodal upper-bound conjecture predicts that the nodal measure of an eigenfunction is bounded by a constant times the square root of its eigenvalue. The formalized counterexample fixes one smooth metric on $S^4\times S^1$ and a sequence of nonzero smooth eigenfunctions whose positive eigenvalues tend to infinity, while the intrinsic four-dimensional nodal measures divided by the square roots of the eigenvalues tend to infinity. The metric is fixed throughout the sequence.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
namespace YauCounterexamples
section
open Set Filter Manifold Bundle MeasureTheory
open scoped Topology ContDiff ENNReal


abbrev Euclidean (n : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin n)
abbrev Sphere (n : ℕ) := Metric.sphere (0 : Euclidean (n + 1)) 1
abbrev FourManifold := Sphere 2 × (Circle × Circle)
abbrev FourModel := Euclidean 2 × (Euclidean 1 × Euclidean 1)

instance torusChartedSpace : ChartedSpace (Euclidean 1 × Euclidean 1) (Circle × Circle) :=
  prodChartedSpace (Euclidean 1) Circle (Euclidean 1) Circle

instance torusIsManifold : IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 1 × Euclidean 1) ∞ (Circle × Circle) := by
  rw [modelWithCornersSelf_prod]
  exact IsManifold.prod Circle Circle

instance fourChartedSpace : ChartedSpace FourModel FourManifold :=
  prodChartedSpace (Euclidean 2) (Sphere 2) (Euclidean 1 × Euclidean 1) (Circle × Circle)

instance fourIsManifold : IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, FourModel) ∞ FourManifold := by
  rw [show 𝓘(ℝ, FourModel) = 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 2).prod
    𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 1 × Euclidean 1) from modelWithCornersSelf_prod]
  exact IsManifold.prod (Sphere 2) (Circle × Circle)

section Definitions
variable {E : Type*} [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
  [FiniteDimensional ℝ E]
variable {M : Type*} [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace E M]
  [IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, E) ∞ M]

/-- Ordinary smooth positive-definite Riemannian metrics, with the existing tangent topology. -/
abbrev SmoothMetric (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    (M : Type*) [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace E M] [IsManifold 𝓘(ℝ, E) ∞ M] :=
  ContMDiffRiemannianMetric 𝓘(ℝ, E) ∞ E (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, E) x)

abbrev CoordIndex (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] := Fin (Module.finrank ℝ E)

/-- A coordinate vector, pushed from a chart back into the tangent bundle. -/
def coordinateVector (p : M) (y : E) (i : CoordIndex E) :
    TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, E) ((chartAt E p).symm y) :=
  mfderiv 𝓘(ℝ, E) 𝓘(ℝ, E) (chartAt E p).symm y (Module.finBasis ℝ E i)

/-- Metric coefficients in a chart. Only values in the chart target are used. -/
def metricCoefficients (g : SmoothMetric E M) (p : M) (y : E) :
    Matrix (CoordIndex E) (CoordIndex E) ℝ :=
  fun i j => g.inner ((chartAt E p).symm y)
    (coordinateVector p y i) (coordinateVector p y j)

/-- The local coordinate formula det(g)^(-1/2) d_i(det(g)^(1/2) g^{ij} d_j u).
The chart at the evaluation point is used, so all derivatives are taken in
its open target. -/
def laplaceBeltrami (g : SmoothMetric E M) (u : M → ℝ) (x : M) : ℝ :=
  let c := chartAt E x
  let a := metricCoefficients g x
  let density := fun y => Real.sqrt (Matrix.det (a y))
  let u' := u ∘ c.symm
  (density (c x))⁻¹ * ∑ i, fderiv ℝ
    (fun y => density y * ∑ j, (a y)⁻¹ i j *
      fderiv ℝ u' y (Module.finBasis ℝ E j))
    (c x) (Module.finBasis ℝ E i)

/-- One compact-chart seminorm test for the usual smooth topology of metrics. -/
structure CoordinateTest (E : Type*) [NormedAddCommGroup E] [NormedSpace ℝ E]
    [FiniteDimensional ℝ E] (M : Type*) [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace E M] where
  center : M
  compactSet : Set E
  isCompact : IsCompact compactSet
  inTarget : compactSet ⊆ (chartAt E center).target
  order : ℕ
  row : CoordIndex E
  column : CoordIndex E

/-- The finite compact-chart derivative tests defining a C∞ neighborhood.
A single positive tolerance suffices for the finitely many tests. -/
def IsSmoothNeighborhood (g₀ : SmoothMetric E M) (N : Set (SmoothMetric E M)) : Prop :=
  ∃ tests : List (CoordinateTest E M), ∃ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε ∧
    ∀ g : SmoothMetric E M,
      (∀ t ∈ tests, ∀ y ∈ t.compactSet,
        ‖iteratedFDeriv ℝ t.order
          (fun z => metricCoefficients g t.center z t.row t.column -
            metricCoefficients g₀ t.center z t.row t.column) y‖ < ε) → g ∈ N

/-- Hausdorff measure for the path distance of the actual Riemannian metric,
not the metric inherited from any ambient embedding. -/
def nodalMeasure [T3Space M] (g : SmoothMetric E M) (d : ℝ) (u : M → ℝ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  letI : RiemannianBundle (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, E) x) :=
    ⟨g.toRiemannianMetric⟩
  letI : IsContinuousRiemannianBundle E (fun x : M => TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, E) x) :=
    ⟨⟨g.inner, g.contMDiff.continuous, fun _ _ _ => rfl⟩⟩
  letI : EMetricSpace M := EMetricSpace.ofRiemannianMetric 𝓘(ℝ, E) M
  letI : MeasurableSpace M := borel M
  letI : BorelSpace M := ⟨rfl⟩
  Measure.hausdorffMeasure d {x | u x = 0}

/-- The eigensequence belongs to one fixed metric and has finite nodal measure. -/
def HasUnboundedNodalRatio [T3Space M] (g : SmoothMetric E M) (d : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (u : ℕ → M → ℝ) (eigenvalue : ℕ → ℝ),
    (∀ j, ContMDiff 𝓘(ℝ, E) 𝓘(ℝ, ℝ) ∞ (u j) ∧ u j ≠ 0 ∧ 0 < eigenvalue j ∧
      (∀ x, -laplaceBeltrami g (u j) x = eigenvalue j * u j x) ∧
      nodalMeasure g (d - 1 : ℕ) (u j) ≠ (∞ : ℝ≥0∞)) ∧
    Tendsto eigenvalue atTop atTop ∧
    Tendsto (fun j => (nodalMeasure g (d - 1 : ℕ) (u j)).toReal / Real.sqrt (eigenvalue j))
      atTop atTop

end Definitions

/-- The round metric is the pullback of the Euclidean scalar product under
inclusion of the unit sphere. This predicate specifies exactly that tensor. -/
def IsRound (g : SmoothMetric (Euclidean 3) (Sphere 3)) : Prop :=
  ∀ (x : Sphere 3) (v w : TangentSpace 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 3) x),
    g.inner x v w = inner ℝ
      (mfderiv 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 3) 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 4)
        (fun y : Sphere 3 => (y : Euclidean 4)) x v)
      (mfderiv 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 3) 𝓘(ℝ, Euclidean 4)
        (fun y : Sphere 3 => (y : Euclidean 4)) x w)


end

open Set Filter Manifold Bundle MeasureTheory
open scoped Topology ContDiff ENNReal


theorem sphere_three (gRound : SmoothMetric (Euclidean 3) (Sphere 3))
    (hRound : IsRound gRound) :
    ∀ N : Set (SmoothMetric (Euclidean 3) (Sphere 3)),
      IsSmoothNeighborhood gRound N →
      ∃ g ∈ N, HasUnboundedNodalRatio g 3 := by
  sorry

theorem sphere_two_torus_two :
    ∃ g : SmoothMetric FourModel FourManifold,
      HasUnboundedNodalRatio g 4 := by
  sorry



end YauCounterexamples
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a smooth metric on the three-sphere, arbitrarily close to the round metric in the smooth topology, and a smooth metric on $`S^2\times\mathbb T^2`$ for which sequences of exact real Laplace eigenfunctions have unbounded nodal measure divided by the square root of the eigenvalue. Each sequence belongs to one fixed metric. Thus the upper-bound part of Yau's nodal conjecture fails for smooth metrics in dimensions three and four.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
##### Introduction

<a id="eq:intro-yau"></a>
Let $(M,g)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold of dimension $d$. We write $-\Delta_g u=\lambda u$ for a nonzero real Laplace eigenfunction with $\lambda>0$, and $Z_u=\{x\in M:u(x)=0\}$ for its nodal set. Yau’s nodal conjecture in the smooth category predicts constants $c_g,C_g>0$, depending on this one metric, such that $$\label{eq:intro-yau}
 c_g\sqrt\lambda\le\mathcal H_g^{d-1}(Z_u)
              \le C_g\sqrt\lambda$$ for every such eigenfunction. The distinction between a fixed metric and a metric chosen anew at each eigenvalue is essential. The lower and upper inequalities are also logically separate. This paper disproves the upper inequality in two low-dimensional smooth settings.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $g_*$ be the round metric on $\mathbb S^3$. Every neighborhood of $g_*$ in the $C^\infty$ topology contains a smooth Riemannian metric $g_\infty$ for which there are nonzero smooth real functions $u_j$ and positive numbers $\lambda_j$ satisfying $$-\Delta_{g_\infty}u_j=\lambda_j u_j,\qquad
 \lambda_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad
 \frac{\mathcal H^2_{g_\infty}(Z_{u_j})}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}}
       \longrightarrow\infty.$$*

</div>

<a id="stmt-thm-four"></a>
<div id="thm:four" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *There are a smooth Riemannian metric $g_\infty$ on $S^2\times\mathbb T^2$, nonzero smooth real functions $u_j$, and positive numbers $\lambda_j\to\infty$ such that $$-\Delta_{g_\infty}u_j=\lambda_j u_j,\qquad
 \frac{\mathcal H^3_{g_\infty}(Z_{u_j})}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}}
       \longrightarrow\infty.$$*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives a negative resolution of the upper-bound part of the smooth nodal conjecture already in dimension three, with metrics arbitrarily close to the round sphere. Theorem <a href="#thm:four" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:four">2</a> provides a distinct four-dimensional topology and metric realization. Neither theorem asserts a power-law excess, and neither contradicts the lower-bound part of <a href="#eq:intro-yau" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-yau">[eq:intro-yau]</a>. In dimensions at least five, a separate companion constructs fixed-metric counterexamples with a fixed positive power-law excess <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[OpenAINodalPower2026](#ref-OpenAINodalPower2026), Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 5.6].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Context.

Yau formulated the nodal measure problem for Laplace eigenfunctions on closed manifolds <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Yau1982](#ref-Yau1982)]. Donnelly and Fefferman established both sharp estimates for real-analytic metrics <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[DonnellyFefferman1988](#ref-DonnellyFefferman1988), Theorem 1.2]. For smooth metrics, Hardt and Simon obtained a general non-polynomial upper estimate <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HardtSimon1989](#ref-HardtSimon1989), Section 5]. Logunov later proved a polynomial upper estimate in dimensions at least three <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LogunovUpper2018](#ref-LogunovUpper2018)], as well as the sharp lower estimate <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[LogunovLower2018](#ref-LogunovLower2018), Theorem 1.1]. The gap between polynomial growth and the conjectured square-root upper bound is where the examples here lie.

The smooth surface case has a separate quantitative history. Brüning proved the sharp square-root lower estimate <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Bruning1978](#ref-Bruning1978), Satz 2]. Donnelly and Fefferman obtained the upper estimate $C_g\lambda^{3/4}$ <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DonnellyFefferman1990](#ref-DonnellyFefferman1990)], and Logunov and Malinnikova improved its exponent to $3/4-\beta$ for a universal $\beta\in(0,1/4)$ <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[LogunovMalinnikova2018](#ref-LogunovMalinnikova2018)]. Under intermediate regularity hypotheses, Hezari proved bounds for Gevrey metrics and a specified quasianalytic class <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Hezari2023](#ref-Hezari2023), preprint version, Theorems 1.1 and 1.2]; these are not statements for arbitrary smooth metrics.

Metric flexibility has also been used to prescribe nodal geometry. On a closed manifold of dimension at least three, Enciso and Peralta-Salas realized any smooth connected closed orientable separating hypersurface as the nodal set of a first nonconstant eigenfunction by choosing the metric <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[EncisoPeraltaSalas2015](#ref-EncisoPeraltaSalas2015), preprint version, Theorem 1.1]. Their work with Steinerberger prescribes any smooth connected closed separating hypersurface in the same dimensions, within each conformal class and with fixed total volume, up to an ambient diffeomorphism arbitrarily close to the identity in $C^0$ <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[EncisoPeraltaSalasSteinerberger2017](#ref-EncisoPeraltaSalasSteinerberger2017), preprint version, Theorem 1.1]. On a fixed round sphere or flat torus, high-energy eigenfunctions can have nodal components of prescribed complicated topology <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[EncisoPeraltaSalasTorres2018](#ref-EncisoPeraltaSalasTorres2018)]. Krauz has prescribed individual nodal configurations on the sphere by metric choice <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Krauz2025](#ref-Krauz2025)]. These results address nodal topology, including at high frequencies on fixed analytic metrics. The distinction in Theorems <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and <a href="#thm:four" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:four">2</a> is the unbounded normalized nodal measure along a sequence of eigenfunctions for one fixed smooth metric.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Two constructions and their common logic.

Each branch starts from a background metric with explicit high-frequency eigenfunctions and a region left unchanged by all local corrections. A smooth logarithmic envelope is corrugated in transverse directions so that its integrated gradient becomes arbitrarily large without losing the strict Hessian inequality needed for localized complex waves. Finite phase and transport jets give quasimodes with arbitrarily small weighted residual. This construction is related to complex-phase WKB quasimodes <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[DenckerSjostrandZworski2004](#ref-DenckerSjostrandZworski2004), Section 3]; the weighted finite-order estimates required here are proved in the wave sections. A Gaussian superposition makes the real value and first derivative jointly nonvanishing and supplies many strict opposite-sign tests along short coordinate segments. These tests form a lower semicontinuous score bounded by the nodal Hausdorff measure, even when the zero set is not assumed regular. Random-wave studies of typical nodal volume provide a related probabilistic backdrop <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[RudnickWigman2007](#ref-RudnickWigman2007), [Wigman2008](#ref-Wigman2008)]; the argument here selects one wave with a large stable sign score and proves its realization under a metric change, rather than importing a random-wave nodal-volume formula.

The obstacle is to replace each quasimode by an exact eigenfunction of an *ordinary* Laplace–Beltrami operator while retaining every sign. In the three-sphere branch, a scalar conductivity and independent density first correct the equation. Division by a positive solution of a semilinear elliptic equation makes them satisfy the three-dimensional metric determinant identity in the active region; a transverse tensor change repairs the remaining defect in a clean annulus. In the $S^2\times\mathbb T^2$ branch, a conformal scalar correction has coercive linearization $\Delta_h-2\lambda$, and a change tangent to the level sets enforces the four-dimensional determinant identity. These are separate exactification arguments, not a dimension-independent realization lemma.

Figure <a href="#fig:two-repairs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:two-repairs">1</a> isolates the two dimension-specific repairs. The first converts a scalar conductivity and density into a metric; the second uses a conformal correction before repairing a rank-three tangent bundle. In both rows, the last tensor change annihilates the gradient of the chosen eigenfunction and therefore leaves its corrected equation unchanged.

<figure id="fig:two-repairs">

<figcaption>Two routes from localized quasimodes to exact eigenfunctions of ordinary metrics. The repairs preserve the strict sign tests used later to pass to one fixed limiting metric.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-two-repairs"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:two-repairs`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 3 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Flowchart diagram with two horizontal rows of rectangular nodes connected sequentially from left to right by horizontal arrows. The top row contains five boxes: "three-sphere quasimode" $\rightarrow$ "scalar weighted correction" $\rightarrow$ "positive factor and division" $\rightarrow$ "two-plane repair" $\rightarrow$ "exact metric eigenpair". The bottom row contains five boxes: "$S^2 \times \mathbb{T}^2$ quasimode" $\rightarrow$ "tensor weighted correction" $\rightarrow$ "conformal factor" $\rightarrow$ "three-plane repair" $\rightarrow$ "exact metric eigenpair".
```


Finally, a perturbation preserving the selected eigenfunction makes its eigenvalue simple. A strict lower bound for the sign score then persists for all metrics in a neighborhood. Nested metric neighborhoods retain each previous witness while inserting the next one, and converge to the single smooth metric required by each theorem. The large-frequency thresholds may depend on each fixed envelope; no uniform spectral gap is required. Uhlenbeck’s generic simplicity theorem <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Uhlenbeck1976](#ref-Uhlenbeck1976)] is context for this spectral step, not a substitute for the selected-mode-preserving perturbations proved here.

Taking the product of the three-sphere metric with any fixed closed Riemannian manifold and pulling back its eigenfunctions gives further qualitative failures in higher dimensions. This elementary extension does not assert a power-law excess in dimensions three or four.

Together with the surface theorem <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OpenAINodalSurfaces2026](#ref-OpenAINodalSurfaces2026), Theorem 1.1], Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and this product observation give the exact universal dimension threshold: among dimensions $d\ge2$, the upper inequality in <a href="#eq:intro-yau" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-yau">[eq:intro-yau]</a>, with a metric-dependent constant uniform over all nonzero real eigenfunctions of positive eigenvalue, holds for every closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold precisely when $d=2$. For each $d\ge3$, there is one fixed smooth metric with a violating sequence.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Organization and conventions.

The three-sphere proof first constructs amplifiable profiles in Section <a href="#sec:profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:profiles">2</a>, waves and signs in Section 3, an exact ordinary metric in Section 4, and a fixed-metric limit in Sections 5–6. The $S^2\times\mathbb T^2$ proof then develops its own envelopes in Section 7, waves in Section 8, sign score in Section 9, metric correction in Section 10, and spectral limit in Section 11. Throughout, gradients and divergences carry the displayed metric unless stated otherwise. Hausdorff measure is taken for the Riemannian distance, with a fixed standard normalization. All finite derivative and residual orders are chosen before sending the frequency to infinity.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
### The three-sphere construction

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
##### Profiles with large mean gradient

A smooth real function $\phi$ will determine the amplitude envelope $e^{n\phi}$ at frequency $n$ for the waves in Section 3. Their construction requires a strict Hessian inequality, whereas their eventual nodal-area lower bound grows with the integrated gradient of $\phi$. We show that these two requirements are compatible: a local perturbation can increase the gradient integral arbitrarily while preserving the Hessian condition and changing $\phi$ arbitrarily little in $C^0$. We then apply this construction on $\mathbb S^3$, preserving a prescribed radial profile outside one coordinate cube.

Throughout this section, gradients, Hessians, and norms are computed using a fixed smooth Riemannian metric $g$ on a three-dimensional manifold. Write $$a_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g\phi,\qquad
 L_\phi=(1+|a_\phi|^2)^{1/2},\qquad H_\phi=\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi.$$

<a id="stmt-def-strict-profile"></a>
<div id="def:strict-profile" class="definition">

**Definition 3** (Strict and full profiles). A smooth function $\phi$ is *strict* at a point if there exists a real vector $b$ at that point such that <a id="eq:profile-strict"></a>$$b\perp a_\phi,\qquad |b|=L_\phi,\qquad
 H_\phi(a_\phi,a_\phi)+H_\phi(b,b)>0.
 \label{eq:profile-strict}$$ It is *full* at that point if the last inequality holds for every $b$ satisfying the first two conditions.

</div>

For a symmetric form $H$ and a unit vector $v\perp a$, it is convenient to set $$\mathcal T(H,a;v)=H(a,a)+(1+|a|^2)H(v,v).$$ Fullness on a compact set where $a_\phi$ does not vanish gives a positive minimum of $\mathcal T(H_\phi,a_\phi;v)$ over the transverse unit-circle bundle. That minimum is allowed to depend on the profile.

The main construction in this section is local.

<a id="stmt-prop-profile-amplification"></a>
<div id="prop:profile-amplification" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Amplification of the mean gradient). *Let $\Omega$ be a coordinate cube with compact closure in a smooth three-dimensional Riemannian manifold. Suppose that $\phi$ is smooth on a neighborhood of $\overline\Omega$, is strict there, and satisfies $a_\phi\ne0$ there. Given $T>0$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth function $\widehat\phi$ on the same neighborhood such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\widehat\phi-\phi)\Subset\Omega,\qquad
 \|\widehat\phi-\phi\|_{C^0(\Omega)}<\varepsilon,
 \qquad
 \int_\Omega |a_{\widehat\phi}|\,\,\mathrm dV_g>T.$$ The function $\widehat\phi$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\overline\Omega$.*

*More precisely, there is a constant $\eta>0$, determined only by the fixed periodic functions chosen below, such that one finite cycle of the construction multiplies $\int_\Omega|a_\phi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$ by at least $1+\eta$. The cycle can have arbitrarily small $C^0$ size. Its patch sizes and frequency thresholds may depend on the entire incoming profile and metric.*

</div>

The cycle has two parts. A first perturbation makes the profile full on a fixed positive fraction of its gradient mass while changing the gradient arbitrarily little. A radial perturbation in the two transverse variables then increases the gradient on a fixed fraction of that region. The radial perturbation preserves strictness, allowing the cycle to be repeated.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Localization and periodic averaging

We use two elementary facts in both parts of the construction.

<a id="stmt-lem-profile-localization"></a>
<div id="lem:profile-localization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Disjoint patches and averaging). *Let $U$ be an open subset of a relatively compact coordinate cube, and let $\mu$ be a finite measure given by a positive smooth density.*

1.  *Given an open neighborhood of each point of $U$, one can choose finitely many coordinate cubes $P_j$, with disjoint interiors and closures contained in those neighborhoods and in $U$, and functions $0\le\chi_j\le1$ in $C_c^\infty(P_j)$, such that $\chi_j=1$ on open cores $C_j\Subset P_j$ and $$\mu\Bigl(\bigcup_j C_j\Bigr)\ge\frac12\mu(U).$$ In particular, the supports of the $\chi_j$ are pairwise disjoint.*

2.  *Suppose that $y$ is a smooth submersion to $\mathbb R^q$, with $q=1$ or $2$, on a neighborhood of $\overline P$, and that $C\Subset P$ is measurable. Let $E\subset\mathbb R^q$ be periodic with respect to a full lattice, have boundary of measure zero in a period cell, and occupy a fraction $p$ of that cell. Then $$\lim_{N\to\infty}
      \mu\{x\in C:Ny(x)\in E\}=p\,\mu(C).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-profile-localization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-profile-localization</summary>

*Proof.* For the first assertion, choose a compact subset of $U$ carrying more than three quarters of its mass. A finite subcover of the prescribed neighborhoods has a Lebesgue number on this compact set. A sufficiently fine coordinate grid therefore gives finitely many cubes, each with closure in one of the prescribed neighborhoods and in $U$, whose union contains that compact set except for grid faces. The faces have zero mass. Shrinking the cores inside these cubes loses arbitrarily little mass, and smooth cutoffs supported in the cubes can be chosen to equal one on the cores.

For the second assertion, submersion coordinates and a finite partition of unity express the pushforward of $\mu|_C$ under $y$ as an integrable density on $\mathbb R^q$. Thus it suffices to average the bounded periodic function $\mathbf 1_E(N\cdot)$ against an $L^1$ function. For the indicator of a rectangle, the assertion follows by tiling with period cells and estimating the cells meeting the rectangle’s boundary. Approximation of an $L^1$ function by finite linear combinations of rectangle indicators proves the claim. The boundary assumption on $E$ ensures that its cell average is well defined and unchanged by choices on its boundary. ◻

</details>

We fix a smooth function $h$ of period one such that $$h''(t)=2\cos(2\pi t).$$ For example, $h(t)=-(2\pi^2)^{-1}\cos(2\pi t)$. The periodic set $$E_h=\{t:h''(t)>1\}$$ has fraction $p_h=1/3$ in each period.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### Preparing a full region

<a id="stmt-lem-profile-preparation"></a>
<div id="lem:profile-preparation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:prepared-mass"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Preparation). *Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-amplification">4</a>, let $0<\epsilon_p\le1/3$ and $\varepsilon>0$. There is a smooth strict function $\psi$, with nonvanishing gradient on $\overline\Omega$, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\psi-\phi)\Subset\Omega,\qquad
 \|\psi-\phi\|_{C^0(\Omega)}<\varepsilon,
 \qquad |a_\psi-a_\phi|\le\epsilon_p|a_\phi|
 \quad\hbox{on }\overline\Omega.$$ Its full region $U\subset\Omega$ satisfies $$\int_U|a_\psi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g
 \ge\frac{p_h}{8}\int_\Omega|a_\psi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g.
 \label{eq:prepared-mass}$$ The change $\psi-\phi$ can, in addition, be made arbitrarily small in $C^1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-profile-preparation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-profile-preparation</summary>

*Proof.* Put $a=a_\phi$. At each point, choose a strict unit direction $v\perp a$. Because $a$ does not vanish, the flow-box construction gives a local first integral $s$, with $\,\mathrm ds(a)=0$, whose differential at the point is any prescribed nonzero covector annihilating $a$. Prescribe it also to annihilate $v$. On a sufficiently small neighborhood, the line $$a^\perp\cap\ker\,\mathrm ds$$ continues to be strict, with a positive margin on a smaller compact neighborhood. The differential $\,\mathrm ds$ remains nonzero there.

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-localization">5</a> to these neighborhoods with $\,\mathrm d\mu=|a|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$. It gives finitely many cubes and cutoff-one cores capturing at least half the mass. We first describe the perturbation on one cube, suppressing its index. Let $\chi$ be its cutoff. For a constant $C>0$ to be fixed before $N$, set $$\psi_N=\phi+C N^{-2}\chi h(Ns).$$ Direct differentiation gives, uniformly on the patch, $$\begin{aligned}
 a_{\psi_N}
 &=a+\frac C N\chi h'(Ns)\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}s
       +\frac C{N^2}h(Ns)\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}\chi,\\
 H_{\psi_N}
 &=H_\phi+C\chi h''(Ns)\,\,\mathrm ds\otimes\,\mathrm ds+O(N^{-1}).
\end{aligned}$$ All constants in this proof may depend on the fixed patch data, including $C$, but not on $N$.

For large $N$, choose a unit vector $v_N$ on the line $$a_{\psi_N}^{\perp}\cap\ker\,\mathrm ds.$$ This line tends uniformly to the old strict line. The added leading Hessian has zero value on $v_N$, while $\,\mathrm ds(a_{\psi_N})=O(N^{-1})$. Its value on the new gradient is therefore $O(N^{-2})$, even on the portions where $h''$ is negative. The remaining Hessian error is $O(N^{-1})$. The old strict margin consequently preserves strictness for all sufficiently large $N$.

We choose $C$ to obtain fullness on the cutoff-one core where $Ns\in E_h$. For the old gradient, consider the symmetric form on $a^\perp$ $$B=(1+|a|^2)H_\phi|_{a^\perp}+H_\phi(a,a)\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}.$$ In an orthonormal basis $(v,w)$ with $\,\mathrm ds(v)=0$ and $w=\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}s/|\,\mathrm ds|$, its $vv$ entry is bounded below by some $m>0$. All its entries are bounded on the patch. Adding $$C(1+|a|^2)|\,\mathrm ds|^2\,w\otimes w$$ makes $B$ uniformly positive definite when $C$ is sufficiently large. Indeed, if $|B_{vw}|\le M$ and $B_{ww}\ge-M$, it is enough to make the new $ww$ entry exceed $M^2/m+m$; the Schur complement is then positive. The factor $(1+|a|^2)|\,\mathrm ds|^2$ has a positive patch minimum, so a finite $C$ suffices. On the specified core $\chi=1$ and $h''>1$, so the actual leading addition is at least this large. Changing the gradient by $O(N^{-1})$ and adding the Hessian remainder preserves its positive minimum for large $N$. Thus $\psi_N$ is full there.

Perform these perturbations on the finite disjoint patches, with their chosen constants $C$, using a common sufficiently large $N$. The changes are arbitrarily small in $C^1$, preserve nonvanishing, and have compact support in $\Omega$. Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-localization">5</a> shows that the union $G_N$ of the cutoff-one regions with $Ns\in E_h$ satisfies $$\int_{G_N}|a|\,\,\mathrm dV_g
 \ge\frac{p_h}{4}\int_\Omega|a|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$$ for sufficiently large $N$. Choose $N$ still larger so that the stated pointwise relative gradient bound holds. With $\mathcal M(\zeta)=\int_\Omega|a_\zeta|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$, the full region therefore has mass at least $$(1-\epsilon_p)\frac{p_h}{4}\mathcal M(\phi)
 \ge\frac{p_h(1-\epsilon_p)}{4(1+\epsilon_p)}\mathcal M(\psi_N)
 \ge\frac{p_h}{8}\mathcal M(\psi_N).$$ Fullness is open where the gradient is nonzero, so this gives the asserted open full region. The $C^0$ bound and any additional prescribed $C^1$ smallness follow by increasing $N$ once more. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### Radial corrugation in two transverse variables

Fix numbers $$0<r_0<r_1<r_2<R<\frac14$$ and a smooth nonnegative function $f'$ supported in $(r_0,R)$, with $f'\ge c_1>0$ on $(r_1,r_2)$. Define $f(s)=\int_0^s f'(t)\,\,\mathrm dt$. On every disk of radius $R$ centered at a point of $\mathbb Z^2$, define $F$ by the radial function $f$; extend it by the constant $f(R)$ off these disks. The result is a smooth $\mathbb Z^2$-periodic function, constant near the disk centers and boundaries. Its annular set $$E_F=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb Z^2}
       \{y:r_1<|y-k|<r_2\}$$ has a fixed positive fraction $p_F=\pi(r_2^2-r_1^2)$ in a period cell.

<a id="stmt-lem-radial-corrugation"></a>
<div id="lem:radial-corrugation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:radial-no-loss"></a>
<a id="eq:radial-gain"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Transverse radial corrugation). *There are constants $\delta>0$ and $g_0>0$, depending only on the fixed function $F$, with the following property. Let $\psi$ be full with nonvanishing gradient on a neighborhood of the closure of a relatively compact coordinate patch $P$. Suppose that $y=(y_1,y_2)$ is a smooth submersion there, with $$\,\mathrm dy(a_\psi)=0,
 \qquad\text{both nonzero singular values of }\,\mathrm dy
       \text{ in }[1/2,2].$$ Let $A>0$ be constant and satisfy $1/2\le A/|a_\psi|\le2$, and let $0\le\chi\le1$ belong to $C_c^\infty(P)$. Set $$\Phi_N=\psi+\frac{\delta A}{N}\chi F(Ny).$$ For every sufficiently large integer $N$, the function $\Phi_N$ is strict and has nonvanishing gradient on $\overline P$. Moreover, $\|\Phi_N-\psi\|_{C^0(P)}\to0$. Given any $\epsilon_r>0$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned}
 |a_{\Phi_N}|&\ge(1-\epsilon_r)|a_\psi|
                      &&\text{on }\overline P,\label{eq:radial-no-loss}\\
 |a_{\Phi_N}|&\ge(1+g_0-\epsilon_r)|a_\psi|
                      &&\text{where }\chi=1\text{ and }Ny\in E_F.
                      \label{eq:radial-gain}
\end{aligned}$$ The frequency thresholds may depend on all the fixed patch data and on $\epsilon_r$; the constants $\delta,g_0$ do not.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-radial-corrugation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-radial-corrugation</summary>

*Proof.* Put $a=a_\psi$ and $a_N=a_{\Phi_N}$. When $Ny$ lies in a periodic disk, let $d_R,d_T$ be the radial and angular unit-coefficient combinations of $\,\mathrm dy_1,\,\mathrm dy_2$. The singular-value hypothesis gives $$\frac12\le |d_R|,|d_T|\le2.$$ These covectors need not be orthogonal in the metric. Outside the disks, set the leading derivatives $f',f'',f'/s$ to zero. There, or in the constant central portions of the disks, choose any orthonormal coefficient pair when such notation is needed. Set $f'/s=0$ at the center. Since $f$ is constant near the center, this agrees with its smooth continuation.

<a id="eq:radial-gradient"></a>
<a id="eq:radial-hessian"></a>
<a id="eq:radial-remainder"></a>
The exact gradient formula and the Hessian expansion are $$\begin{aligned}
 a_N&=a+q\,d_R^\sharp+e_N,\\
 q&=\delta A\chi f',\qquad
 e_N=\frac{\delta A}{N}F(Ny)\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}\chi,
 \end{aligned}
 \label{eq:radial-gradient}$$ $$H_{\Phi_N}
 =H_\psi+N\delta A\chi
       \left[f''d_R^2+\frac{f'}s d_T^2\right]+E_N,
 \label{eq:radial-hessian}$$ with $$|E_N|\le C_P\left((\chi+|\,\mathrm d\chi|)f'+N^{-1}\right).
 \label{eq:radial-remainder}$$ Here and below $C_P$ denotes a finite constant depending on the fixed patch data, independent of $N$. To check the remainder, its terms are $$\begin{aligned}
 &\delta A\chi\sum_i F_i(Ny)\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}y_i,\\
 &\delta A\left(\,\mathrm d\chi\otimes\sum_i F_i(Ny)\,\mathrm dy_i
       +\sum_i F_i(Ny)\,\mathrm dy_i\otimes\,\mathrm d\chi\right),\\
 &\frac{\delta A}{N}F(Ny)\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}\chi.
\end{aligned}$$ Since $|(F_1,F_2)|=f'$, these give Equation <a href="#eq:radial-remainder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-remainder">[eq:radial-remainder]</a>. In the constant portions of $F$, its value contributes only to $e_N$ and to the last displayed term.

<a id="eq:radial-leading-trace"></a>
We first control the leading Hessian trace in <a href="#eq:radial-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-hessian">[eq:radial-hessian]</a>. The leading increment in <a href="#eq:radial-gradient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-gradient">[eq:radial-gradient]</a> is perpendicular to $a$, so $$|a_N|\ge |a|-|e_N|.$$ Thus $a_N$ is nonzero for large $N$. Choose a vector $b_N$ satisfying $$b_N\perp a_N,\qquad d_R(b_N)=0,\qquad
 |b_N|=L_N:=(1+|a_N|^2)^{1/2}.$$ The two linear constraints have a nonzero common solution in dimension three. They imply $$\langle a,b_N\rangle=-\langle e_N,b_N\rangle.$$ Because $|a|$ has a positive patch minimum, the component of $b_N/L_N$ along $a$ tends uniformly to zero. The kernel of $\,\mathrm dy$ is precisely the line spanned by $a$. Its singular-value lower bound and the identity $d_R(b_N)=0$ consequently give, for all sufficiently large $N$, $$|d_T(b_N)|=|\,\mathrm dy(b_N)|\ge\frac14 L_N.$$ Also $A\le3L_N$ for large $N$, and $$|d_R(a_N)|\le4\delta A\chi f'+2|e_N|.$$ With $K=\|f''\|_\infty$ and $B_N=f''d_R^2+(f'/s)d_T^2$, it follows that $$\begin{aligned}
 B_N(a_N,a_N)+B_N(b_N,b_N)
 &\ge\frac1{16}L_N^2\frac{f'}s-K|d_R(a_N)|^2\\
 &\ge\frac1{16}L_N^2\frac{f'}s
       -288K\delta^2L_N^2(f')^2-8K|e_N|^2.
\end{aligned}$$ The estimate uses $(x+y)^2\le2x^2+2y^2$; in particular, its final error is $O_P(N^{-2})$. Write $S_0=\sup_s sf'(s)>0$. Choose once and for all $$0<\delta^2\le\frac1{9216KS_0}.$$ Then $(f')^2\le S_0 f'/s$ gives $$\begin{split}
 N\delta A\chi
   \bigl[B_N(a_N,a_N)+B_N(b_N,b_N)\bigr]
 \ge N\delta A\chi\frac{L_N^2}{32}\frac{f'}s
       -C_P N^{-1}.
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:radial-leading-trace}$$ All constants used to choose $\delta$ depend only on $F$ and the numerical singular-value bounds.

<a id="eq:radial-small-product"></a>
We next control the transition where the favorable leading term can be small. A nonnegative smooth cutoff satisfies $$|\,\mathrm d\chi|\le C_P\sqrt\chi.$$ Indeed, extend it by zero in the coordinate chart. If its Euclidean Hessian is bounded by $M>0$, the Taylor inequality at $x-M^{-1}D\chi(x)$ gives $|D\chi(x)|^2\le2M\chi(x)$; metric equivalence gives the stated bound. Consequently, with $t=\chi f'$, Equations <a href="#eq:radial-gradient" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-gradient">[eq:radial-gradient]</a> and <a href="#eq:radial-remainder" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-remainder">[eq:radial-remainder]</a> imply $$|a_N-a|\le C_P(t+N^{-1}),\qquad
 |E_N|\le C_P(t+\sqrt t+N^{-1}).
 \label{eq:radial-small-product}$$

Let $m>0$ be the minimum of the full trace $\mathcal T(H_\psi,a;v)$ over the transverse unit circles on $\overline P$. Project $b_N/L_N$ onto $a^\perp$ and normalize; for large $N$ this gives a unit vector $v_N\perp a$ differing from $b_N/L_N$ by $O_P(N^{-1})$. Using also $|L_N-L_\psi|\le|a_N-a|$, the base Hessian trace is at least $$H_\psi(a_N,a_N)+H_\psi(b_N,b_N)
 \ge m-C_P(t+N^{-1}).$$ The vectors $a_N,b_N$ are uniformly bounded for this fixed patch, so the trace of the remainder in <a href="#eq:radial-small-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-small-product">[eq:radial-small-product]</a> is bounded in absolute value by $C_P(t+\sqrt t+N^{-1})$. Choose $t_0>0$ sufficiently small that these terms involving $t$ and $\sqrt t$ total less than $m/4$ when $t\le t_0$. For sufficiently large $N$, Equation <a href="#eq:radial-leading-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-leading-trace">[eq:radial-leading-trace]</a> now gives a strictly positive total trace throughout $t\le t_0$.

On the complementary region $t\ge t_0$, one has $s\le R$, and the positive term in <a href="#eq:radial-leading-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-leading-trace">[eq:radial-leading-trace]</a> is at least $$\frac{N\delta A t_0}{32R}.$$ It tends to infinity and dominates the bounded base and remainder traces. This proves strictness for all sufficiently large $N$. In this argument $t_0$ and the frequency threshold can depend on the full margin $m$ and all patch derivatives. The choice of $\delta$ is already fixed. In particular, the region $t\to0$ requires no lower bound for the product $Nt$.

Finally, exact orthogonality in the leading gradient gives $$|a+q d_R^\sharp|^2=|a|^2+q^2|d_R|^2.$$ The uniform error $e_N=O_P(N^{-1})$ proves <a href="#eq:radial-no-loss" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-no-loss">[eq:radial-no-loss]</a>. Where $\chi=1$ and $Ny\in E_F$, the bounds $A\ge|a|/2$ and $|d_R|\ge1/2$ give $$|a+q d_R^\sharp|
 \ge |a|\sqrt{1+\frac{\delta^2c_1^2}{16}}.$$ Set $$g_0=\sqrt{1+\frac{\delta^2c_1^2}{16}}-1>0.$$ Decreasing the fixed $\delta$ if necessary, assume $g_0\le1$. The positive minimum of $|a|$ allows $|e_N|\le\epsilon_r|a|$ for large $N$, proving <a href="#eq:radial-gain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:radial-gain">[eq:radial-gain]</a>. The $C^0$ convergence follows directly from the factor $N^{-1}$ in the definition of $\Phi_N$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
###### A fixed gain and finite iteration

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-radial-corrugation-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-radial-corrugation</summary>

*Proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-amplification">4</a>.* Keep the periodic functions and the constants from Lemma <a href="#lem:radial-corrugation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:radial-corrugation">7</a> fixed. Define $$\theta=\frac{p_h p_F}{64},\qquad
 t_* =\theta g_0,\qquad \eta=\frac{t_*}{4}>0.$$ We prove the cycle statement, allowing any prescribed positive $C^0$ budget for that cycle.

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-preparation">6</a> with $\epsilon_p\le\min(1/3,t_*/16)$ and half the $C^0$ budget. Write $\psi$ for the resulting profile and $U$ for its full region. The preparation, including its frequency, is now held fixed. On a sufficiently small neighborhood of each point of $U$, the flow-box construction gives two first integrals $y_1,y_2$ of $a_\psi$. Their differentials can be prescribed to form an orthonormal basis of $a_\psi^\perp$ at the center; after shrinking, the nonzero singular values of $\,\mathrm dy$ lie in $[1/2,2]$. One can also choose a constant $A$ on that patch with $1/2\le A/|a_\psi|\le2$.

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-localization">5</a> in $U$ with density $|a_\psi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g$. Its radial patch cores capture at least half the full-region mass. In view of Equation <a href="#eq:prepared-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prepared-mass">[eq:prepared-mass]</a>, their total mass is at least $(p_h/16)\mathcal M(\psi)$. For all sufficiently large common radial frequencies $N$, periodic averaging gives a set of cutoff-one annular points of mass at least $$\frac{p_h p_F}{32}\mathcal M(\psi)
 \ge\theta\mathcal M(\psi).$$ Use Lemma <a href="#lem:radial-corrugation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:radial-corrugation">7</a> on these disjoint patches. Increase $N$ to obtain strictness, nonvanishing, the remaining half of the $C^0$ budget, and relative errors $$\epsilon_r\le\frac{t_*}{8},\qquad \epsilon_r\le\frac{g_0}{2}.$$ Call the resulting profile $\widehat\psi$. On the selected annular set the gradient multiplier is at least $1+g_0/2$; everywhere else it is at least $1-\epsilon_r$. Therefore $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathcal M(\widehat\psi)
 &\ge\left(1-\epsilon_r+\frac{\theta g_0}{2}\right)
          \mathcal M(\psi)\\
 &\ge\left(1+\frac{3t_*}{8}\right)\mathcal M(\psi)\\
 &\ge\left(1-\frac{t_*}{16}\right)
          \left(1+\frac{3t_*}{8}\right)\mathcal M(\phi)\\
 &\ge(1+\eta)\mathcal M(\phi).
\end{aligned}$$ The last inequality uses $0<t_*\le1$. This establishes a gain independent of the incoming profile and of the full-region patch sizes. The latter are fixed only after preparation; their finite bounds determine how large the radial frequency must be.

Since $\mathcal M(\phi)>0$, finitely many cycles raise it above $T$. Divide $\varepsilon$ among those cycles. Each cycle has compact support in $\Omega$ and preserves strictness and nonvanishing on $\overline\Omega$. Their finite union of supports is compactly contained in $\Omega$, and their total $C^0$ change is less than $\varepsilon$. All functions produced are smooth. This completes the proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-amplification">4</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
###### Profiles on the round sphere

We record the global profiles used below, including the behavior at their critical points. On the unit sphere $\mathbb S^3\subset\mathbb R^4$, let $g_*$ be the round metric and set $$r=(x_1^2+x_2^2)^{1/2}.$$ Fix a coordinate cube $\Omega$ whose closure lies in $1/20<r<1/8$, and fix its coordinate Lebesgue measure $\,\mathrm dx$.

<a id="stmt-prop-spherical-profiles"></a>
<div id="prop:spherical-profiles" class="proposition">

**Proposition 8** (Spherical profiles). *There is a $C^2$ neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of $g_*$ such that the following holds. For every smooth $g\in\mathcal U$, every $1/5\le t\le3/10$, and every $T,\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth real function $\phi$ on $\mathbb S^3$ with $$\phi_0=\log t+r^2-t^2,\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(\phi-\phi_0)\Subset\Omega,
 \qquad \|\phi-\phi_0\|_{C^0}<\varepsilon,$$ such that*

1.  *$\phi$ is strict on $r\le3/10$;*

2.  *its critical points in this region are exactly $r=0$, and at each such point $H_\phi$ is positive on a two-plane;*

3.  *$\phi>\log r$ for $0<r<t$, while $\phi<\log r$ for $t<r\le1$;*

4.  *$\displaystyle\int_\Omega |a_\phi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g>T$.*

<a id="eq:profile-coordinate-mass"></a>
*The metric-coordinate comparison constants on $\Omega$, including the Lipschitz constants of its two-coordinate projections for the ambient Riemannian distance, can be chosen uniformly for $g\in\mathcal U$. In particular, $$\int_\Omega L_\phi\,\,\mathrm dx
 \ge c\int_\Omega |a_\phi|\,\,\mathrm dV_g
 \label{eq:profile-coordinate-mass}$$ with one constant $c>0$ for this neighborhood. Each constructed profile is fixed before a wave frequency is chosen.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-spherical-profiles"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-spherical-profiles</summary>

<a id="eq:round-profile-hessian"></a>
*Proof.* The function $\phi_0$ is smooth on the sphere. Its round Hessian is $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_{g_*}\phi_0
 =2(\,\mathrm dx_1^2+\,\mathrm dx_2^2)-2r^2g_*.
 \label{eq:round-profile-hessian}$$ The eigenvalues of $\,\mathrm dx_1^2+\,\mathrm dx_2^2$ on the tangent three-plane are $1$, $1-r^2$, and $0$: its two nonzero eigenvalues are those of the Gram matrix $\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}_2-(x_1,x_2)^T(x_1,x_2)$. The Hessian eigenvalues in <a href="#eq:round-profile-hessian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:round-profile-hessian">[eq:round-profile-hessian]</a> are consequently $$2-2r^2,\qquad 2-4r^2,\qquad -2r^2.$$ On $r\le3/10$, the first two exceed $1$, and the third exceeds $-1/2$. These inequalities persist uniformly in a sufficiently small $C^2$ neighborhood $\mathcal U$ of the round metric. The derivatives of $\phi_0$ do not depend on $t$, so the same neighborhood works for all the stated $t$.

For such a metric, intersect a positive two-plane of $H_{\phi_0}$ with $a_{\phi_0}^\perp$ and choose a unit vector $v$ in the intersection. Then $$\mathcal T(H_{\phi_0},a_{\phi_0};v)
 \ge-\frac12|a_{\phi_0}|^2+1+|a_{\phi_0}|^2>0.$$ Thus $\phi_0$ is strict throughout $r\le3/10$. Also $$|\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_{g_*}(r^2)|^2=4r^2(1-r^2).$$ Vanishing of a differential is independent of the metric, so $\phi_0$ has nonvanishing gradient for $0<r<1$, in particular on a neighborhood of $\overline\Omega$.

For the envelope comparison, put $$j_t(r)=\log t+r^2-t^2-\log r.$$ One has $j_t(t)=0$ and $j_t'(r)=2r-r^{-1}$. Hence $j_t(r)>0$ for $0<r<t$. On $t<r\le1$, the only possible interior critical point is a minimum, while $j_t(1)=\log t+1-t^2<0$ for the stated range of $t$; thus $j_t<0$ there. Compactness gives a positive lower bound for $j_t$ on $\overline\Omega$, uniform over $t\in[1/5,3/10]$.

Apply Proposition <a href="#prop:profile-amplification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:profile-amplification">4</a> to $\phi_0$ on $\Omega$, using a $C^0$ tolerance smaller than both $\varepsilon$ and half this positive lower bound. Extend the perturbation by zero on the sphere. It yields the desired integral, preserves the envelope inequalities, and preserves strictness and nonvanishing in $\Omega$. Outside $\Omega$ the profile is unchanged, so it is strict throughout $r\le3/10$ and has no critical points there except $r=0$.

At $r=0$, the perturbation vanishes on a neighborhood and $\,\mathrm d\phi_0=0$. The connection term in the Hessian therefore vanishes, and for every incoming metric $$H_\phi=2(\,\mathrm dx_1^2+\,\mathrm dx_2^2)
 \qquad\text{at }r=0.$$ This form is positive semidefinite of rank two. It is positive on a two-plane, and every two-plane contains a direction on which it is strictly positive. This last observation will allow transverse phase directions to be chosen even at critical points.

Finally, shrink $\mathcal U$ if necessary so that the metric and coordinate norms, and the coordinate volume densities on $\overline\Omega$, are uniformly comparable. Since $L_\phi\ge|a_\phi|$, the uniform upper bound for $\,\mathrm dV_g/\,\mathrm dx$ proves <a href="#eq:profile-coordinate-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:profile-coordinate-mass">[eq:profile-coordinate-mass]</a>. Each coordinate function on $\overline\Omega$ extends smoothly to the sphere using a cutoff in its chart. The extensions have uniformly bounded gradients for $g\in\mathcal U$, so their two-coordinate projections have uniform Lipschitz constants for the ambient Riemannian distance. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:waves"></a>
##### Finite complex waves and robust nodal-area witnesses

This section converts a fixed strict profile into a real approximate eigenfunction. Its weighted residual can be made smaller than any prescribed inverse power of the frequency, while its value and gradient cannot vanish together. The same function has a nodal-area witness whose constant is independent of the size and derivatives of the profile. Proposition <a href="#prop:wave-realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:wave-realization">14</a> collects these three conclusions for one realization.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
###### Setting and the critical-point hypothesis

Fix the coordinate cube $\Omega$ and the smooth coordinate extensions used in Proposition <a href="#prop:spherical-profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spherical-profiles">8</a>. Choose a sufficiently small metric neighborhood $\mathcal G$ of the round metric inside $\mathcal U$. The coordinate and Riemannian norms on $\overline\Omega$ are uniformly equivalent for $g\in\mathcal G$, and the two-coordinate projections have the uniform Lipschitz bounds for the ambient Riemannian distance established there. We fix this neighborhood and these coordinates for the rest of the argument.

<a id="eq:wave-envelope-margin"></a>
In this section the incoming metric $g\in\mathcal G$ is smooth and equals $g_*$ for $r\ge R_0$. Fix radii $$\frac15\le R_0<t<t_1<t_2<\frac3{10}.$$ The fixed smooth profile $\phi$ equals $$\phi_0=\log t+r^2-t^2$$ off a compact subset of $\Omega$, has no critical point in $\overline\Omega$, and satisfies $$\phi>\log r\quad(0<r\le R_0).
 \label{eq:wave-envelope-margin}$$ Put $a=\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g\phi$ and $L=(1+|a|_g^2)^{1/2}$. On $K_1=\{r\le t_1\}$ we impose the following pointwise hypotheses:

<a id="eq:wave-strict-hypothesis"></a>
1.  At a noncritical point there is a vector $b$ such that $$b\perp a,\qquad |b|_g=L,\qquad
     \mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi(a,a)+\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi(b,b)>0.
     \label{eq:wave-strict-hypothesis}$$

2.  At a critical point, $\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi$ is positive definite on some two-dimensional subspace of the tangent space.

The second hypothesis is stronger than the existential inequality at $a=0$. It is available for the profiles used here: all their critical points in $K_1$ lie on $r=0$, where the perturbations vanish and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_g\phi=2(dx_1^2+dx_2^2).$$ Here $d\phi=0$, so this Hessian identity is independent of the connection. The form is positive semidefinite of rank two. At every other point outside the perturbations with $r<1$, $d(r^2)\ne0$. Thus the conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:spherical-profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:spherical-profiles">8</a> supply exactly the hypotheses above.

All profile data, the incoming metric, and the radii are fixed before the integer $n$ is chosen. Constants in phase estimates may depend on these fixed data and on finitely many requested derivative orders. The constants in the final sign and area estimates will depend only on the fixed coordinate and metric bounds.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
###### Complex Hessians below the profile

Complex tangent vectors and symmetric forms are paired bilinearly in the phase equations. Transpose therefore means ordinary transpose, without complex conjugation.

<a id="stmt-lem-phase-hessian"></a>
<div id="lem:phase-hessian" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:phase-hessian-conditions"></a>
**Lemma 9** (Admissible phase Hessians). *Let $H$ be a real symmetric form on a three-dimensional Euclidean space, and let $z=a+ib$, where $a,b$ are real, $a\perp b$, and $|b|^2=1+|a|^2$. If $a\ne0$ and $H(a,a)+H(b,b)>0$, there is a complex symmetric form $Q$ such that $$Qz=0,\qquad \Re Q<H.
 \label{eq:phase-hessian-conditions}$$ If $a=0$, the same conclusion follows from $H(b,b)>0$. Moreover, a choice satisfying <a href="#eq:phase-hessian-conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:phase-hessian-conditions">[eq:phase-hessian-conditions]</a> continues with locally bounded coefficients and a positive real-part gap under small changes of $H,z$ with $z\cdot z=-1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-phase-hessian"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-phase-hessian</summary>

*Proof.* Write $Q=U+iD$ with $U,D$ real symmetric. The equation $Qz=0$ is equivalent to $$Db=Ua,\qquad Da=-Ub.$$ For $a\ne0$, these prescribed columns of a symmetric $D$ are compatible exactly when $U(a,a)+U(b,b)=0$. To see sufficiency explicitly, write $a=Ae_1$, $b=Be_2$ in an orthonormal frame. Prescribe $$De_1=-\frac BA Ue_2,\qquad De_2=\frac AB Ue_1.$$ Their overlapping off-diagonal entries agree precisely when $A^2U_{11}+B^2U_{22}=0$; symmetry prescribes the remaining entries in the first two rows, and $D_{33}$ is free. Taking $$\alpha=\frac{H(a,a)+H(b,b)}{|a|^2+|b|^2}>0,
 \qquad U=H-\alpha\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$$ therefore proves the assertion.

If $a=0$, then $|b|=1$. Set $U=0$ on $\mathbb Rb$ and $U=-M\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}$ on $b^\perp$, with no mixed entries, and take $D=0$. In a frame starting with $b$, the first diagonal entry of $H-U$ is $H(b,b)>0$. Increasing $M$ makes its transverse Schur complement positive definite. Hence $H-U>0$ and $Qb=0$.

For the continuation assertion, start with $Qz=0$ and $z\cdot z=-1$. For nearby $z'$ with $z'\cdot z'=-1$, define $$P(z')=\mathop{\mathrm{Id}}+z'(z')^T,
 \qquad Q'=P(z')^TQP(z').$$ Then $P(z')z'=0$, so $Q'$ is symmetric and annihilates $z'$. At $z'=z$ one has $QP(z)=P(z)Q=Q$, and hence $Q'=Q$. Although $P(z')$ is singular, these identities suffice: continuity preserves the strict inequality $H'-\Re Q'>0$ for nearby $H',z'$. No division by $|a|$ is involved in this continuation. ◻

</details>

For each $p\in K_1$, choose a unit vector $\nu$ with $a(p)=|a(p)|\nu$; at a critical point any unit $\nu$ is allowed. There are two linearly independent vectors $b_0,b_1\in\nu^\perp$, both of length $L(p)$, for which the strict inequality in <a href="#eq:wave-strict-hypothesis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:wave-strict-hypothesis">[eq:wave-strict-hypothesis]</a> holds. At a noncritical point, the good directions form a nonempty open subset of the transverse circle.
