Weak unique continuation for smooth scalar elliptic equations on a connected manifold, a consequence of Aronszajn’s Theorem <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Aronszajn1957](#ref-Aronszajn1957), pp. 235–236], makes it identically zero. The hypotheses apply because the metric is smooth and positive and the zeroth-order coefficient $\Lambda_k$ is smooth. This contradicts the choice of $t_2$ and proves the claim.

<a id="spec:positive-variation"></a>
On a small neighborhood of a point supplied by the claim, let $\Pi$ be the $g$-orthogonal projection onto $\ker dt$ and put $z=\Pi\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g t_2$. Choose a nonzero nonnegative smooth bump $\chi$ supported in that neighborhood, with $z\ne0$ on its support. Extend by zero the endomorphism <a id="spec:tangential-variation"></a>$$J=\chi\left(z\otimes z^{\flat_g}-\frac{|z|_g^2}{3}\Pi\right).
 \label{spec:tangential-variation}$$ It is smooth, $g$-self-adjoint, trace free, and annihilates $\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g t$. Here the factor $1/3$ uses $\operatorname{rank}\Pi=3$. Moreover, $$\mathfrak b_J(t_2,t_2)
 :=\int_M g(J\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g t_2,\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g t_2)\,
                  d\operatorname{vol}_g
 =\frac23\int_M\chi|z|_g^4\,d\operatorname{vol}_g>0.
 \label{spec:positive-variation}$$ For real $s$ define an ordinary metric by <a id="spec:metric-path"></a>$$g_s^{-1}=\exp(sJ)g^{-1}.
 \label{spec:metric-path}$$ Self-adjointness makes this a positive symmetric cometric. Since $\det\exp(sJ)=\exp(s\operatorname{tr}J)=1$, its volume density equals that of $g$. Since $J\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g t=0$, it also satisfies $g_s^{-1}dt=g^{-1}dt$. The divergence formula for the Laplacian therefore gives $$-\Delta_{g_s}t=\Lambda_k t
 \quad\hbox{for every }s.$$ Thus both volume and the flux of $dt$ are preserved exactly.

We show that the multiplicity at $\Lambda_k$ is less than $m$ for all sufficiently small nonzero $s$. Suppose instead that $s_n\to0$, $s_n\ne0$, and that there are $m$ real orthonormal eigenfunctions $e_{1,n},\ldots,e_{m,n}$ of $g_{s_n}$ at $\Lambda_k$. All norms and orthogonality may be taken with the same volume density. Smooth convergence of the metrics, elliptic estimates, and a diagonal compactness argument give a subsequence such that $e_{a,n}\to e_a$ smoothly for $1\le a\le m$. To spell out the uniform estimate used here, for every fixed integer $r\ge0$, comparison with the limiting operator and absorption of its small coefficient difference give $$\|v\|_{H^{r+2}}
 \le C_r\bigl(\|\Delta_{g_{s_n}}v\|_{H^r}
                         +\|v\|_{L^2}\bigr)$$ for all sufficiently large $n$. Iteration at the fixed eigenvalue gives uniform Sobolev bounds of every order; compact Sobolev embeddings give the asserted convergence. The limits form an orthonormal basis of $E$.

For $v\in E$, the weak equation for $e_{a,n}$ with metric $g_{s_n}$ and the weak equation for $v$ with metric $g$ have the same right-hand side. Subtracting and dividing by $s_n$ yields $$\int_M g\left(\frac{\exp(s_nJ)-I}{s_n}\mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g e_{a,n},
                    \mathop{\mathrm{grad}}_g v\right)\,d\operatorname{vol}_g=0.$$ Taking the limit shows $\mathfrak b_J(e_a,v)=0$ for all $a$ and all $v\in E$. Since the $e_a$ span $E$, this contradicts <a href="#spec:positive-variation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:positive-variation">[spec:positive-variation]</a>. This proves the strict decrease.

The eigenfunction $t$ survives every decrease, so the multiplicity is always at least one. At most $m-1$ repetitions make it one. At each repetition the parameter can be taken arbitrarily small in the smooth topology. For example, choose a finite $C^r$ tolerance contained in the prescribed neighborhood and allocate to these finitely many changes a total tolerance smaller than it. Their supports form a finite union of compact subsets of $P$, leaving another background region whenever one is needed. Volume and $t$ are preserved throughout. This proves all the assertions. ◻

</details>

<a id="spec:score-comparison"></a>
Fix a constant $c_{\mathrm H}>0$ such that the comparison in Lemma <a href="#score:measure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="score:measure">32</a> reads $$\mathcal H_g^3(\{v=0\})\ge c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(v),\qquad g\in\mathcal U,
 \label{spec:score-comparison}$$ for every continuous real $v$ and every sign score used in the construction. Its uniformity follows from the fixed metric comparability on $\mathcal U$. When considering persistence, the cubes and displacements defining $\mathcal S$ are held fixed.

<a id="stmt-spec-persistence"></a>
<div id="spec:persistence" class="lemma">

**Lemma 40** (Open persistence of a fixed score). *Suppose $g\in\mathcal U$ has a simple positive eigenvalue $\lambda$ with real eigenfunction $t$, and, for a fixed sign score and $N>0$, $$\lambda>N,\qquad c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t)>N\sqrt\lambda.$$ There is a smooth-topology neighborhood $\mathcal V\subset\mathcal U$ of $g$ such that every $g'\in\mathcal V$ has a simple eigenvalue $\lambda'>N$ with a real eigenfunction $t'$ satisfying $$c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t')>N\sqrt{\lambda'}.$$ In particular its nodal-measure ratio exceeds $N$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-persistence"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-persistence</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a number $T$ and an open interval $I$ containing $\lambda$ such that $$N\sqrt\lambda<T<c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t),
 \qquad I\subset\bigl(N,(T/N)^2\bigr).$$ These choices are possible by the two strict inequalities in the hypothesis. Normalize $t$ in $L^2(g)$; scalar invariance leaves its score unchanged. The functional $\mathcal F=c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S$ is lower semicontinuous for uniform convergence and invariant under nonzero real rescaling by <a href="#score:lsc" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="score:lsc">[score:lsc]</a> and the paragraph following it. Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:simple-eigenpair-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:simple-eigenpair-continuity">23</a> to this functional, the threshold $T$, and the interval $I$, and intersect its neighborhood with $\mathcal U$. For every metric $g'$ in the resulting neighborhood, the selected simple eigenpair satisfies $$\lambda'\in I,\qquad c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t')>T
                         >N\sqrt{\lambda'}.$$ In particular $\lambda'>N$, and <a href="#spec:score-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:score-comparison">[spec:score-comparison]</a> gives the nodal-measure conclusion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-spec-one-step"></a>
<div id="spec:one-step" class="proposition">

**Proposition 41** (An arbitrarily close step). *Let $h\in\mathcal U$ and suppose $h-g_0$ has compact support in $P$. For every smooth-topology neighborhood $\mathcal O$ of $h$, every $N>0$, and every integer $k_{\min}$, there are an integer $k\ge k_{\min}$, a metric $\widetilde h\in\mathcal O\cap\mathcal U$, and a real eigenfunction $t$ such that $$-\Delta_{\widetilde h}t=\Lambda_k t,\qquad
 \Lambda_k>N,\qquad c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t)>N\sqrt{\Lambda_k}$$ for a sign score $\mathcal S$, and $\Lambda_k$ is simple. Both $\widetilde h-g_0$ and $t-u_k^0$ have compact support in $P$. The inequalities persist in a neighborhood of $\widetilde h$ in the sense of Lemma <a href="#spec:persistence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:persistence">40</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-one-step"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-one-step</summary>

*Proof.* First choose a finite $C^r$ neighborhood of $h$ contained in $\mathcal O\cap\mathcal U$, reserving part of its tolerance for Lemma <a href="#spec:simple" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:simple">39</a>. Let $c_{\mathrm s}>0$ be the constant, independent of the amplified envelope, in the conclusion of Proposition <a href="#score:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="score:good">34</a>. By Lemma <a href="#env:gaps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="env:gaps">27</a>, choose the nested patches and an envelope with the required gaps and with $$c_{\mathrm H}c_{\mathrm s}\int_D B(x)\,dx>2\sqrt3\,N.$$ For these fixed data, Lemma <a href="#corr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="corr:realization">35</a> specifies finite residual derivative orders and accuracy powers that suffice for the reserved $C^r$ tolerance. Construct the packets to those orders and use Proposition <a href="#score:good" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="score:good">34</a>, for arbitrarily large integers $k$, to obtain a real $u$ satisfying the residual and noncancellation hypotheses as well as $$\mathcal S(u)\ge c_{\mathrm s}k\int_D B(x)\,dx.$$ Choose $k\ge k_{\min}$ with $\Lambda_k>N$ above all these thresholds and those required by the correction. All order choices precede this choice of $k$. Lemma <a href="#corr:realization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="corr:realization">35</a> gives an ordinary metric $g_*$ within the reserved tolerance and an exact $\Lambda_k$ eigenfunction $t$, a positive smooth multiple of $u$, with the stated background identities outside a compact subset of $P$. Positive-factor invariance gives $\mathcal S(t)=\mathcal S(u)$. Since $\sqrt{\Lambda_k}=\sqrt{2k^2+k}\le\sqrt3\,k$ for $k\ge1$, $$c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S(t)>2N\sqrt{\Lambda_k}.$$ Lemma <a href="#spec:simple" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:simple">39</a> then makes $\Lambda_k$ simple by an arbitrarily small change preserving $t$. Take this change within the remaining tolerance. All support conditions are preserved, and Lemma <a href="#spec:persistence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:persistence">40</a> supplies the last claim. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-one-step-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-one-step</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:four" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:four">2</a>.* Let $\mathcal X=C^\infty(M;\operatorname{Sym}^2T^*M)$, the real Fréchet space of smooth symmetric covariant two-tensors. Fix $C^r$ seminorms defined using $g_0$ and the complete translation-invariant metric $$d_{\mathcal X}(a,b)
 =\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}2^{-r-1}
                 \min\{1,\|a-b\|_{C^r}\}.$$ It induces the smooth topology. Completeness follows because a Cauchy sequence converges in every $C^r$ norm and the limits agree under the natural inclusions. The positive metrics form an open subset of $\mathcal X$, and $\mathcal U$ is open in this ambient space.

Take any sequence $N_j\to\infty$, with $N_j>0$. Starting from $g_0$, apply Proposition <a href="#spec:one-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:one-step">41</a> with threshold $N_1$ to choose a first center $h_1$ and its persistence neighborhood $\mathcal V_1\subset\mathcal U$. Choose a closed $d_{\mathcal X}$-ball $\mathcal B_1$ of positive radius centered at $h_1$, contained in $\mathcal V_1$, and of diameter at most $2^{-1}$. Inductively, suppose $h_{j-1}$ and $\mathcal B_{j-1}$ have been chosen. Its interior is a neighborhood of $h_{j-1}$. Proposition <a href="#spec:one-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:one-step">41</a>, with threshold $N_j$, therefore gives a center $$h_j\in\operatorname{int}\mathcal B_{j-1}$$ whose difference from $g_0$ has compact support in $P$, together with a persistence neighborhood $\mathcal V_j$. Choose a positive-radius closed ball centered at $h_j$ such that $$\mathcal B_j\subset
 \operatorname{int}\mathcal B_{j-1}\cap\mathcal V_j\cap\mathcal U,
 \qquad \operatorname{diam}\mathcal B_j\le2^{-j}.$$ This is possible because the set on the right is open and contains $h_j$. Every open neighborhood in the smooth topology contains a neighborhood specified by finitely many seminorms, exactly the closeness provided by Proposition <a href="#spec:one-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:one-step">41</a>.

These are nested nonempty closed sets with diameters tending to zero in the complete space $\mathcal X$. Their centers are Cauchy, and their limit $g_\infty$ belongs to every $\mathcal B_j$. In particular $g_\infty\in\mathcal U$, so it is a smooth positive Riemannian metric on the fixed closed connected four-manifold $M=S^2\times\mathbb T^2$. For each $j$, membership in $\mathcal V_j$ supplies an exact nonzero real eigenfunction $u_j$ of this same metric, with eigenvalue $\lambda_j$ satisfying $$\lambda_j>N_j,\qquad
 \frac{\mathcal H_{g_\infty}^3(\{u_j=0\})}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}}
 \ge\frac{c_{\mathrm H}\mathcal S_j(u_j)}{\sqrt{\lambda_j}}>N_j.$$ Here $\mathcal S_j$ is the fixed score from the $j$th step. Both quantities therefore tend to infinity. Compact support in $P$ was needed only for the finite-stage centers; it is not a hypothesis on the limit used in this conclusion. Likewise the final eigenvalues may move from the intermediate values $\Lambda_k$: persistence gives the exact eigenpairs and the two strict inequalities at the single limiting metric. This proves the stated refutation. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "Two routes from localized quasimodes to exact eigenfunctions of ordinary metrics. The repairs preserve the strict sign tests used later to pass to one fixed limiting metric.",
      "figure_id": "fig:two-repairs",
      "pdf_page": 3,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:two-repairs",
          "exact_quote": "Two routes from localized quasimodes to exact eigenfunctions of\nordinary metrics. The repairs preserve the strict sign tests used later\nto pass to one fixed limiting metric.",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  every node/.style={draw,align=center,font=\\footnotesize,minimum height=10mm,text width=25mm},\n  >=Latex,node distance=2.2mm]\n  \\node (a) {three-sphere\\\\quasimode};\n  \\node[right=of a] (b) {scalar weighted\\\\correction};\n  \\node[right=of b] (c) {positive factor\\\\and division};\n  \\node[right=of c] (d) {two-plane\\\\repair};\n  \\node[right=of d] (e) {exact metric\\\\eigenpair};\n  \\node[below=7mm of a] (f) {$S^2\\times\\T^2$\\\\quasimode};\n  \\node[right=of f] (g) {tensor weighted\\\\correction};\n  \\node[right=of g] (h) {conformal\\\\factor};\n  \\node[right=of h] (i) {three-plane\\\\repair};\n  \\node[right=of i] (j) {exact metric\\\\eigenpair};\n  \\foreach \\x/\\y in {a/b,b/c,c/d,d/e,f/g,g/h,h/i,i/j}\n    \\draw[->] (\\x) -- (\\y);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "Flowchart diagram with two horizontal rows of rectangular nodes connected sequentially from left to right by horizontal arrows. The top row contains five boxes: \"three-sphere quasimode\" $\\rightarrow$ \"scalar weighted correction\" $\\rightarrow$ \"positive factor and division\" $\\rightarrow$ \"two-plane repair\" $\\rightarrow$ \"exact metric eigenpair\". The bottom row contains five boxes: \"$S^2 \\times \\mathbb{T}^2$ quasimode\" $\\rightarrow$ \"tensor weighted correction\" $\\rightarrow$ \"conformal factor\" $\\rightarrow$ \"three-plane repair\" $\\rightarrow$ \"exact metric eigenpair\"."
    }
  ],
